Capture is installed, verified across sessions, and committed.

## 1. Setup

- **Tool:** Claude Code 2.1.273 (VS Code extension, Windows 11)
- **Model:** `claude-opus-5` for both planning and execution — no planner/executor split
- **Automatic mechanism:** Yes. Claude Code has a first-class hooks system. I checked the docs rather than assuming.

## 2. What's installed

Two hooks in [.claude/settings.json](.claude/settings.json), both calling [.claude/hooks/capture.js](.claude/hooks/capture.js):

| Event | Captures |
|---|---|
| `UserPromptSubmit` | `prompt` — verbatim |
| `Stop` | `last_assistant_message` — the final response |

Because `Stop` hands over the assembled final response directly, the log contains the prompt and the final answer and **nothing in between** — no thinking, tool calls, or retries. The config is project-scoped and committed, so it applies to every session started in this repo.

## 3. Verified, not assumed

Canaries ran in **two separate CLI sessions** (`109caa2a`, `29922eea`), neither being the session that installed the hook — that's the part that proves it isn't session-local. Both landed. The setup session itself (`c4110ea9`) also got captured mid-session without a restart. `.agent-logs/.capture-errors.log` is empty.

Full writeup in [CAPTURE-TEST.md](CAPTURE-TEST.md), committed as `f010e3d` alongside the logs.

## 4. Things that went wrong, since they're the useful part

- I delegated a docs lookup, and the report claimed the model name **isn't** in the transcript, recommending `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`. That's wrong — `message.model` is on every assistant entry, and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` isn't set here. Following it would have made every entry read `unknown`. I checked directly instead.
- First working version still emitted `model: unknown` on fresh sessions — transcript flush lag, not a missing field. Fixed with a bounded retry that runs only at `Stop` and only when nothing was found. Canary 1 vs canary 2 shows the before/after; I left both as-is.
- I assumed the end-of-turn hook required transcript parsing (as the brief's wording suggests). Dumping a real payload showed `last_assistant_message` was right there.

Two limitations I've documented rather than papered over: the **first** prompt entry of any session reads `model: unknown` (genuinely unknowable at that instant — the response entry and frontmatter carry the real model), and background-task notifications arrive through `UserPromptSubmit` so they log as prompts. I chose not to filter those, since a filter risks silently dropping a real prompt.

## One thing I need

Step 5 says to start the assignment — but the assignment brief itself wasn't included in what you pasted, only the capture-setup instructions. Send me the actual brief and I'll start; everything from here is recorded automatically.

It's ~30 minutes. Save everything to `/recon` and commit it — that's your evidence.

**Shot list (one screenshot each, desktop + mobile where marked):**

| # | Flow | What to capture |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Home, signed out | Header, nav bar, hero carousel, category card grid |
| 2 | Search autocomplete | Dropdown mid-type, category selector open |
| 3 | Search results | Left filter rail, sort dropdown, Sponsored/Prime/Amazon's Choice badges |
| 4 | Product detail 📱 | Full page: gallery, buy box, bullets, variants, delivery promise |

| 6 | Add to cart | The interstitial/confirmation Amazon shows |
| 7 | Cart 📱 | Line items, qty selector, Save for later, subtotal box |

| 9 | Order confirmation | Delivery date, "Track package" |
| 10 | Orders | Order history list |
| 11 | Sign-up | Both the create-account and sign-in screens |

While you're in there, note the things that carry the feel: exact button colors, how dense the type is, how fast the search dropdown opens, and how the buy box sticks on scroll. I've built the plan below against Amazon's current desktop UI as I know it — send me shots that contradict it and I'll adjust.

---

# A. Core user journeys

Ranked by how much they matter to the demo. Everything works **signed out** — that's requirement 7, and it drives the whole architecture.

1. **Discover → Buy** (the spine): Home → search or category → results → PDP → add to cart → cart → checkout → confirmation. This must be flawless end to end. It's the walkthrough.
2. **Search & refine**: autocomplete → results → filter by price/brand/rating/Prime → sort → paginate.
3. **Evaluate a product**: gallery zoom, variant switching, reviews, delivery estimate, stock state.
4. **Manage cart**: quantity, remove, save for later, live subtotal, free-shipping threshold progress.
5. **Post-purchase**: confirmation with delivery date → order history → order detail.
6. **Optional identity**: mock sign-in that personalizes the greeting and claims the guest cart. Never a gate.

Journeys 1–4 are the product. 5 is cheap and makes it feel complete. 6 is last.

# B. Screens to reproduce

| Route | Screen | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | Home — hero carousel, category grid, "Deals", carousels | P1 |
| `/s?k=` | Search results + facets (doubles as category listing) | P0 |
| `/dp/[slug]` | Product detail | P0 |
| `/cart` | Cart | P0 |
| `/checkout` | Checkout (3 accordion steps, Amazon-style) | P0 |
| `/order/[id]` | Order confirmation | P0 |
| `/orders` | Order history | P2 |
| `/ap/signin`, `/ap/register` | Mock auth | P3 |
| `*` | 404 + empty/loading states | P2 |

Nine routes. `/s` serving both search and category browse is a deliberate collapse — Amazon does the same thing, and it halves the work.

# C. Components to build

**Chrome:** `SiteHeader` (logo, deliver-to, search with category dropdown + autocomplete, account menu, cart badge), `SubNav`, `Footer` (four-column + back-to-top).

**Catalog:** `ProductCard`, `StarRating`, `PriceBlock` (whole/fraction superscript, list-price strikethrough, % off), `Badge` (Prime / Amazon's Choice / Best Seller / Sponsored), `DeliveryPromise` (computes a real date from today), `FilterRail`, `SortSelect`, `Pagination`, `Breadcrumbs`.

**PDP:** `ImageGallery` (thumb rail + hover zoom), `VariantPicker`, `BuyBox` (sticky), `QuantitySelect`, `FeatureBullets`, `ReviewSummary` (histogram), `ReviewList`, `FrequentlyBoughtTogether`.

**Commerce:** `CartLineItem`, `OrderSummaryCard`, `CheckoutStep` (accordion), `AddressForm`, `PaymentForm` (mock, Luhn-validated), `OrderCard`.

**Primitives:** `Button` (Amazon's yellow/orange/grey variants), `Input`, `Select`, `Dialog`, `Popover`, `Accordion`, `Toast`, `Skeleton`, `EmptyState`.

The three that decide whether this looks real: `PriceBlock`, `ProductCard`, `BuyBox`. I'll spend disproportionate time there.

# D. Data model

No database. Seeded TypeScript catalog + browser persistence. Reasoning in section I.

```ts
Category   { id, slug, name, parentId?, image }
Product    { id, slug, title, brand, categoryId, price, listPrice?,
             rating, reviewCount, images[], bullets[], description,
             specs: Record<string,string>, variants?: Variant[],
             stock, isPrime, deliveryDays, badges[] }
Variant    { id, type: 'color'|'size'|'style', label, swatch?, priceDelta, image? }
Review     { id, productId, author, rating, title, body, date, verified, helpfulCount }

CartItem   { productId, variantId?, qty, savedForLater }
Address    { fullName, line1, line2?, city, state, zip, phone }
Payment    { last4, brand, expMonth, expYear }   // never a real number
Order      { id, items[], address, payment, subtotal, shipping, tax,
             total, placedAt, deliveryDate, status }
User       { id, name, email }                    // optional, mock
```

Persistence: `cart`, `orders`, `recentlyViewed`, `user` in `localStorage` via Zustand `persist`. Catalog ships in the bundle and is read server-side for SSR.

Catalog size: **~120 products across 10 categories**, enough that filters and pagination have something to bite on. Generated once with a seeded RNG so ratings/review counts are stable across builds.

# E. Recommended stack

| Layer | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Framework | **Next.js 15, App Router, TypeScript** | SSR for real-feeling search/PDP, file routing matches the URL scheme, deploys to Vercel with zero config |
| Styling | **Tailwind CSS v4** | Amazon's look is custom and dense; utility classes beat fighting a component library's opinions |
| Primitives | **Radix UI** (dialog, popover, select, accordion) | Accessible behavior without imposed visuals |
| State | **Zustand + persist** | Cart/orders in localStorage in ~20 lines. No provider pyramid |
| Search | **Fuse.js** over the seeded catalog | Fuzzy matching and typo tolerance for free; autocomplete behind a route handler so it feels server-backed |
| Icons | **lucide-react** | |
| Fonts | System stack / Inter | Amazon Ember is licensed — don't ship it |
| Deploy | **Vercel** | |

**Rejected:** shadcn/ui (its aesthetic is the opposite of Amazon's density — I'd override everything). A real DB + Prisma + Neon (2–4h for zero visible product). A Vite SPA (faster to start, but loses SSR and the routing story).

# F. Deployment strategy

**Deploy an empty shell to Vercel in hour 1, not hour 23.** A broken build at the end is the single most likely way to fail this, and it costs 15 minutes now.

1. Public GitHub repo, `main` as default.
2. Vercel project linked to the repo; every push to `main` auto-deploys.
3. Push after each phase — always a working public URL.
4. Verify in a private window that it opens with no session.
5. Custom domain only if there's spare time. `*.vercel.app` satisfies "live link."

**Committing:** the `.agent-logs/` hook is already running; I'll commit logs interleaved with the code they produced, per the brief. Screenshots go in `/recon` in the first commit.

# G. Features to exclude — deliberately

**Cut entirely:** real payment processing, real auth/OAuth/passwords, seller and marketplace surfaces, Prime Video/Music/Photos, Alexa, subscriptions and Subscribe & Save, returns/refunds, gift cards, customer service chat, Fresh/Whole Foods, warehouse and shipping-speed logic, ML recommendations, a real ad auction, review submission, i18n and currency switching, wishlists/registries, Q&A sections, price history, comparison tables.

**Keep as convincing illusions** — near-zero cost, high fidelity signal:
- "Sponsored" labels on a couple of results
- Prime badges and "FREE delivery **Tuesday, September 23**" computed from the real current date
- "Amazon's Choice" and "Best Seller" tags
- "Only 3 left in stock — order soon" on low inventory
- Frequently bought together with a bundle price
- A "deliver to" location chip in the header

That list is the product judgment the evaluator is looking for. The instinct to reproduce Amazon's breadth is exactly the trap; a shallow clone of forty features loses to a deep clone of six.

# H. Implementation order

Build a **thin end-to-end slice first**, then deepen. There should never be a moment where the demo doesn't run start to finish.

| Phase | Hours | Deliverable |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0–0.5 | *You:* recon screenshots → `/recon`, committed |
| 1 | 0.5–1.5 | Scaffold, Tailwind design tokens (Amazon palette/type scale), **deployed to Vercel** |
| 2 | 1.5–2.5 | Catalog generator, 120 products, images sourced, types locked |
| 3 | 2.5–4 | Header + SubNav + Footer. The chrome is 60% of the "is this Amazon?" reaction |
| 4 | 4–5 | **Thin slice:** basic results → basic PDP → cart → checkout stub → confirmation. Ugly but complete. Deploy |
| 5 | 5–7 | PDP in full: gallery + zoom, variants, sticky buy box, reviews, FBT |
| 6 | 7–9 | Search: autocomplete, facets, sort, pagination, category browse |
| 7 | 9–10 | Cart: qty, save for later, free-shipping meter, empty state |
| 8 | 10–11 | Checkout: 3-step accordion, validation, confirmation, order history |
| 9 | 11–12.5 | Home page: carousel, category grid, deals rows, recently viewed |
| 10 | 12.5–14 | Polish: responsive, keyboard/a11y, skeletons, empty states, 404, Lighthouse |
| 11 | 14–15 | *You:* walkthrough recording, README, final checks |

**~15 hours of the 24.** The buffer is deliberate — it absorbs the phase that runs long, and every phase after 4 ends on something demoable.

# I. Risks and time-saving decisions

**Risks**

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| **Product images.** Amazon's CDN blocks hotlinking and the images are copyrighted. This sinks the whole visual result if unsolved | Unsplash Source or curated open-licensed images, downloaded into `/public/products` at build time. Never hotlink Amazon |
| **Trademark.** A pixel-perfect Amazon clone on a public URL using the real wordmark is a genuine exposure | Keep the layout and palette; use a distinct wordmark, and put "Educational clone. Not affiliated with Amazon.com, Inc." in the footer. Costs nothing, removes the problem |
| Late build failure | Deploy in hour 1, push every phase |
| Home page scope creep — infinite carousels | Timeboxed to 1.5h in phase 9, and it's last on purpose. It's the least important screen for a shopping demo |
| Reviews feel fake and flatten the PDP | Write ~40 varied reviews by hand, reuse across products with seeded selection |
| Walkthrough overruns 5 min | Script it against journey 1. Rehearse once |

**Time-saving decisions, stated plainly**

- **No database.** localStorage gives persistent carts and order history with zero infra and zero deploy risk. The only thing lost is cross-device sync, which no evaluator will test in a 5-minute walkthrough. Saves ~3h.
- **No real auth.** Mock sign-in that sets a name. Requirement 7 says guest usability, so auth is decoration — build it last, or not at all.
- **`/s` serves both search and category browse.** One screen, two jobs. Saves ~1.5h.
- **Seeded deterministic catalog** rather than hand-authoring 120 products. Saves ~2h.
- **Depth over breadth on the PDP.** It's where shoppers form their judgment and where a clone most obviously succeeds or fails. It gets 2 full hours.

---

## Three decisions I need from you

1. **Vercel account** — do you have one linked to GitHub? If not, that's your 5-minute task during phase 1.
2. **Product images** — Unsplash (real photos, generic) or AI-generated product shots (more on-brand, slower)? I'd go Unsplash.
3. **Branding** — my recommendation is Amazon's exact layout and colors with a distinct wordmark plus a footer disclaimer. Say the word if you'd rather go fully pixel-exact on the logo too, and I'll do that instead.

Confirm and I'll start with phase 1. Send the `/recon` screenshots whenever you have them — they'll sharpen phases 3–5, and they don't block the scaffold.

---

# What the recon changed in my plan

Six concrete corrections. These matter more than the rest of this document.

| # | What I had | What the screenshots show | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Search results as a **grid** of cards | **List rows** — big image left, dense content right, ~16 per page | Rebuild results as rows. Denser, more Amazon, more info per screen |
| 2 | Add to cart only on the PDP | **"Add to cart" pill on every result row and every carousel card** | Big win. Cart fills in one click during the demo |
| 3 | Toast on add to cart | A **dedicated confirmation page** *and* a **right-hand slide-in panel** | Build the slide-in drawer. Distinctive, and it beats a toast |
| 4 | Plain autocomplete dropdown | Dropdown **dims the entire page behind a scrim**, suggestions bold only the completion suffix | Cheap, and instantly recognizable |
| 5 | Generic "on sale" styling | Red `-41%` chip + `Limited time deal` + `Only 8 left in stock - order soon.` | Three tiny elements that do enormous fidelity work |
| 6 | PDP "build it deep" | Real PDP is **15,171px tall** — A+ content, videos, 4 carousels, spec tabs, foreign reviews | Confirms the cut list. Reproducing ~35% gets ~90% of the perceived completeness |

---

# Design tokens (read off the screenshots)

```
bg.page      #EAEDED    header      #131921    subnav    #232F3E
surface      #FFFFFF    border      #D5D9D9    divider   #E7E7E7
text         #0F1111    muted       #565959    link      #007185
link.hover   #C7511F    star        #DE7921    success   #007600

search.btn   #FEBD69 (hover #F3A847)
```

Type scale `12 / 13 / 14 / 16 / 18 / 21 / 28`. Body **14px**. Amazon Ember is licensed — **Inter** is the closest free substitute. Spacing on a 4px grid; card padding 14–20px; 16px grey gutter between white cards. Border radius: 8px cards/inputs, **full pill** for all buttons.

---

# Implementation spec

### 1. Header — MUST
Two rows, sticky on scroll. Row 1 (`#131921`, 60px): wordmark · "Deliver to / United States" with pin (small grey line over bold white) · search bar · "EN ▾" · "Hello, sign in / Account & Lists ▾" · "Returns / & Orders" · cart icon with orange count badge + "Cart".

Search bar = category `All ▾` select (`#E6E6E6`) + input + `#FEBD69` button with magnifier, one rounded 8px group. Focus ring is a **3px orange glow** around the whole group.

- **SHOULD:** account dropdown on hover, "Deliver to" opens a zip modal.
- **CUT:** language switcher (render it, make it inert).

### 2. Navigation — MUST
Row 2 (`#232F3E`, 39px): `☰ All` then Today's Deals · Customer Service · Registry · Gift Cards · Sell. Hover = 1px white outline inset.
- **SHOULD:** `☰ All` opens a left slide-in department drawer with a scrim.
- **CUT:** mega-menus, Prime Video/Coupons (no destination).

### 3. Homepage — MUST
Grey page, white cards, 1500px max width. Order: hero banner carousel (chevrons, ~4 slides) → **4-up category card row** → full-width **horizontal product carousel** → 4-up card row → carousel. Alternating, exactly as recon.

Category card = white, bold 18px title, one large image **or** a 2×2 image grid with a caption under each, blue link at the bottom ("Shop gaming" / "See all deals" / "Discover more").

- **SHOULD:** "Best Sellers in …" carousels, "Your recently viewed items".
- **CUT:** international/locale modules, sponsored brand rows, the 9-row homepage — **4–5 sections is plenty**.

### 4. Search experience — MUST
Focus or typing triggers a **full-page dark scrim** (`rgba(0,0,0,.5)`, below the header) plus a white dropdown pinned under the input. Up to 10 suggestions, ~28px rows, hover `#F7FAFA`. **Typed prefix in regular weight, completion in bold.** Debounce 120ms. `↑ ↓` to move, `Enter` to search, `Esc` to close.
- **SHOULD:** "in Electronics" category-scoped suffix on some rows; recent searches when the input is empty.
- **CUT:** thumbnails in suggestions, server-side ranking.

### 5. Search results — MUST
Toolbar: `1-16 of over 1,000 results for "laptop"` with the query **bold and orange**, right-aligned `Sort by: Featured ▾`. Then `Results` (21px bold) + grey subline.

Each row: 240px image cell · content column containing → `Sponsored ⓘ` (a few only) · title (17px, blue on hover, clamp 2) · grey subtitle · rating row (`4.5` + stars + `▾` + blue count) · social proof (`50+ bought in past month`) · **price block** · strikethrough list price · `FREE delivery **Tue, Sep 23**` with a bold date · red stock warning · **yellow "Add to cart" pill** · `+2 other colors/patterns`.

- **SHOULD:** pagination, "no results" empty state, grid/list toggle.
- **CUT:** infinite scroll, real ad auction.

### 6. Filters — MUST
240px left rail, sticky. Groups separated by hairlines, each a bold 15px heading + rows: **Customer Reviews** (star rows, "& Up"), **Price** (brackets + a min/max box), **Brand** (checkboxes), **Deals**, one category-specific facet (e.g. RAM Size). Filters write to the URL query so state is shareable and back/forward works.
- **SHOULD:** "Popular Shopping Ideas" text-link chips + `⌄ See more` disclosure; applied-filter pills with clear-all.
- **CUT:** dynamic facet counts, cross-facet availability logic.

### 7. Product detail — MUST
Breadcrumbs, then 3 columns: **gallery** (vertical thumb rail, hover-to-zoom lens) · **center** (title 24px, `Visit the X Store` link, rating + blue review count, hairline, price block + `-41%` chip + list price, variant swatches, `About this item` bullets) · **sticky buy box** (price, `FREE delivery **Tuesday, September 23**`, green `In Stock`, Qty select, **yellow Add to cart**, **orange Buy Now**, 🔒 Secure transaction, Ships from / Sold by).

Below: spec table · "Products related to this item" carousel · reviews (histogram bars + `Customers say` AI-ish summary + highlight chips + 4–6 review cards with avatar, stars, title, date, `Verified Purchase`, Helpful button).

- **SHOULD:** Frequently Bought Together bundle; image lightbox.
- **CUT:** A+ marketing content, videos, "Looking for specific info?", other-sellers box, reviews from other countries, Q&A, similar brands.

### 8. Cart — MUST
Left white card: `Shopping Cart` (28px) + right-aligned grey `Price` label. Each line: 180px thumb · title (clamp 2) · bold variant label · green `In Stock` · **qty stepper pill** (`🗑 | 1 | +`, orange border) · blue action row `Delete | Save for later | Compare | Share` · right-aligned price with deal chip and strikethrough. Footer: right-aligned `Subtotal (N items): $X`.

Right rail card: `Subtotal (N items)` bold, `This order contains a gift` checkbox, **yellow Proceed to checkout** pill.

**Slide-in drawer** on add-to-cart: green ✓ Added to cart, thumb, subtotal, `Go to Cart`, auto-dismiss.

- **SHOULD:** Save for later section, free-shipping progress meter, empty-cart state.
- **CUT:** the full "Added to Cart" interstitial page (the drawer supersedes it), gift wrap, Compare/Share (render inert).

### 9. Checkout — MUST ⚠️ *no recon — standard pattern*
Stripped header (wordmark + `Secure checkout` + lock, **no nav or search** — this focus trick reads as real). Three numbered accordion steps: **1 Shipping address** → **2 Payment method** → **3 Review items and shipping**. Completed steps collapse to a summary + blue `Change`. Right rail order-summary card sticks throughout: Items / Shipping / Estimated tax / **Order total** in red bold, yellow `Place your order`.

Payment is **mock and clearly labelled** — Luhn-checked card field, no processing, no storage. Then `/order/[id]` confirmation: green ✓, delivery date, summary, `Track package`.

- **SHOULD:** order history at `/orders`; address form validation with inline errors.
- **CUT:** real payments, gift cards/promo codes, delivery-speed options, address book.

### 10. Responsive — MUST ⚠️ *no mobile recon*
Breakpoints 640 / 768 / 1024 / 1280. Below `lg`: search drops to its own full-width row under the logo; filters move into a `Filters` bottom-sheet; results go single-column; PDP stacks gallery → title → price → **Add to cart** → bullets; cart summary moves above the line items; buy box becomes a sticky bottom bar. Tap targets ≥ 44px.
- **CUT:** a separate mobile homepage; hover-zoom on touch (use the lightbox instead).

### 11. Typography — MUST
| Use | Size / weight |
|---|---|
| Page title (`Shopping Cart`) | 28 / 400 |
| Section (`Results`) | 21 / 700 |
| PDP title | 24 / 400 |
| Card & filter headings | 18 / 700 · 15 / 700 |
| Body / dense | 14 / 400 · 13 / 400 |
| Meta, legal | 12 / 400 `#565959` |

**Price block is the signature and must be exact:** `$` at 13px raised, integer at 28px, decimals at 13px raised, all baseline-aligned via `<sup>`. Get this wrong and nothing else reads as Amazon.

### 12. Spacing — MUST
4px grid. 16px grey gutter between cards; 14–20px card padding; 12px between list rows plus a hairline; 8px label→control; 1500px container with 16px side gutters. Dense by intent — Amazon's small gaps are the point, and generous whitespace will read as "not Amazon".

### 13. Card patterns — MUST
Three variants of one `ProductCard`:
- **Carousel** — square image, blue 2-line title, stars + count, price, optional red deal chip, `Add to cart` pill.
- **Result row** — horizontal, as §5.
- **Rail mini** — small thumb left, text right, used in cart sidebar and recently-viewed.

Plus the **category card** (§3). All: white, 8px radius, `#D5D9D9` border, hover raises shadow slightly.

### 14. Buttons — MUST
| Variant | Fill | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Primary | `#FFD814` | Add to cart, Proceed to checkout, Place your order |
| Secondary | `#FFA41C` | Buy Now |
| Outline | white / `#D5D9D9` | Go to Cart, See buying options |
| Search | `#FEBD69` | search submit |

All **fully rounded pills**, 1px darker border, subtle inner highlight, `active:` pressed-inset shadow. Heights 28 (compact) / 32 (default) / 44 (mobile).

### 15. Micro-interactions
**MUST** — search scrim + bold-suffix suggestions · cart badge count animates on add · slide-in cart drawer · PDP gallery hover-zoom · qty stepper shows a **trash icon at qty 1** · link hover → orange + underline · sticky buy box · skeleton loaders on search.

**SHOULD** — carousel chevrons fade in on hover · "Only N left" pulses once · optimistic cart updates · `✓ Added` flash on the row button · back-to-top bar.

**CUT** — page transitions, parallax, confetti, anything that costs more than 15 minutes.

---

# Branding

Wordmark proposal: **Kartly** — lowercase, with an amber swoosh underline arcing from `k` to `y` (our own analog of a smile-arrow, not a copy of it). Alternates: **Zentra**, **Bazario**. Keep the navy/yellow commerce palette, which is convention rather than anyone's property. Footer carries: *"Kartly is an original demo storefront. Not affiliated with Amazon.com, Inc."*

---

# Final prioritized implementation checklist

**P0 — the demo does not exist without these**
1. Scaffold Next.js 15 + TS + Tailwind v4, tokens above, deploy to Vercel **in hour 1**
2. Seeded catalog: 120 products, 10 categories, Unsplash imagery downloaded to `/public`
3. `PriceBlock`, `StarRating`, `Button`, `Badge` — the fidelity primitives
4. Header (2 rows, sticky) + subnav + footer + `Kartly` wordmark
5. Search results as **list rows**, with Add-to-cart on every row
6. PDP: gallery, price, variants, bullets, sticky buy box
7. Cart: line items, qty stepper, subtotal rail, slide-in drawer
8. Checkout: 3 accordions + stripped header → order confirmation
9. **Deploy and verify in a private window**

**P1 — this is what "complete" looks like**
10. Autocomplete with page scrim + bold-suffix suggestions
11. Filter rail wired to URL params + sort dropdown + pagination
12. Deal chips, stock warnings, delivery dates computed from today
13. Homepage: hero carousel + 2 card rows + 2 product carousels
14. Reviews: histogram + summary chips + review cards
15. Responsive down to 375px
16. Empty states, skeletons, 404

**P2 — only if the clock allows**
17. Save for later · recently viewed · free-shipping meter
18. Order history at `/orders`
19. Department drawer behind `☰ All`
20. Frequently Bought Together
21. Mock sign-in that personalizes the greeting

**Explicitly cut:** real auth · real payments · any backend or database · A+ content · videos · Q&A · multi-seller · gift cards · promo codes · i18n · recommendation ML.

---

P0 #1–2 complete, tested and committed.

## Commits

| Hash | What |
|---|---|
| `b380d7c` | Phase 0 recon screenshots |
| **`e3e08d7`** | **P0 #1–2 — foundation + catalog** |

## Verification (all run locally, nothing deployed)

```
npm run typecheck   exit 0
npm run build       compiled in 13.4s, 0 errors, 3 static pages
npm run start       home 200 in 12ms · 404 route 200→404 correctly
                    image optimisation 200 (29KB avif/jpeg)
                    12 cards · 8 deal chips · 2 low-stock warnings
                    delivery dates computed live (Sep 20 → "Thu, Sep 24")
```

## What's in place

**Foundation** — Next 16 App Router + TS + Tailwind v4. Design tokens in [globals.css](app/globals.css) taken off the screenshots rather than guessed. `splitPrice()` in [utils.ts](lib/utils.ts#L12) implements the raised-symbol / large-whole / raised-decimal price format, which is the detail that most decides whether a listing reads as real.

**Catalog** — 120 products, 10 departments, 672 reviews, [deterministically generated](scripts/generate-catalog.mjs) so rebuilds produce no diff. Typed access layer in [catalog.ts](lib/catalog.ts).

## Three judgment calls worth flagging

1. **Invented brand names** (Aureon, Nordvik, Kestrel…). Real brand names carry the same trademark problem as copying a real logo, and fictional ones read just as convincingly.

2. **Rating histograms are solved, not faked.** My first version gave a 4.6-star product *zero* 1–3 star reviews — a clean gradient that instantly looks synthetic. Real distributions are J-shaped, so I binary-search an exponential decay until the weighted mean matches the displayed rating. A 4.6 product now reads 73/18/5/2/1, max error 0.04 across all 120.

3. **Images: 130/130 from Openverse, CC0/Public-Domain-Mark only** — no attribution obligation, though [ATTRIBUTION.md](ATTRIBUTION.md) credits them anyway. Downloaded into `/public` and committed, so production never calls a third-party image host. 7MB total.

**One tradeoff to be aware of:** CC0 photos are *lifestyle* shots, not white-background studio product shots. The laptop image is a real laptop on a desk, not a cutout on white. `object-contain` on white handles this well and it's what a real marketplace does with mixed-ratio imagery, but it will read slightly more "curated marketplace" than "catalogue". I think that's the right trade versus spending hours on background removal — flagging it now rather than at QA.

## Notes

- `localhost` resolves to IPv6 here and Next binds IPv4 — use `127.0.0.1:3000`.
- Next auto-generated `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` and adjusted `tsconfig.json`; both committed.
- [app/page.tsx](app/page.tsx) is an explicitly temporary foundation-check page. It gets replaced by the real homepage at P1 #13.
- Deployment deferred per your instruction. Vercel needs no config for this stack — `next build` already passes, which is the same command Vercel runs.

Ready for **P0 #3–4** (fidelity primitives, then header/subnav/footer). Say go.

**Product** — [ProductCard](components/product/ProductCard.tsx) with **grid / row / mini** layouts, plus [AddToCartButton](components/product/AddToCartButton.tsx). One card serves every listing surface, so price and badge treatment can't drift between pages.

**State** — [cart store](lib/store/cart.ts): zustand + localStorage, guest-first.

## 2. Header functionality

Two-row sticky header, **60px + 39px**, search bar **40px** — measured by decoding `recon/filter.png` rather than eyeballing. The screenshot's 71px row turned out to be 125% Windows display scaling in your capture; 40 and 39 land exactly on standard values at 1.25×, which is what let me pin the real numbers.

Row 1: wordmark · Deliver to · department select + search + Go · EN · Account & Lists · Returns & Orders · cart with live count. Row 2: `☰ All` drawer · shortcuts · departments · delivery line.

Search submits by **Enter** and by **button**, scopes by department, and round-trips through the URL (`/s?k=laptop` repopulates the field).

## 3. Mobile behaviour

A genuinely separate layout, not a shrunk desktop: 50px row (hamburger · wordmark · Sign in · cart) with **search on its own full-width row** beneath, then the subnav scrolling horizontally. Drawer slides from the left over a scrim, closes on Escape, backdrop click, or navigation; body scroll locks while open. **No horizontal overflow at 1440, 768 or 390.**

## 4. Build status

```
npm run typecheck   exit 0
npm run build       compiled clean, 3 routes
Playwright QA       problems: []
```
Zero console errors, zero page errors, zero unexpected 404s **against the production build**.

## 5. Commit

**`6abbbe2`** — P0 #3–4: fidelity primitives and marketplace chrome (110 files)

## 6. Remaining issues

**Three real bugs I found by driving a browser rather than trusting the markup** — worth noting because none were visible from the HTML:
- **Search never submitted.** The form had no `action`, so any submit before hydration went nowhere. Now a real GET form to `/s` with JS as progressive enhancement.
- **Cart badge pinned to 0 forever.** I set the hydration flag by mutating state inside `onRehydrateStorage`, which never notifies subscribers. Replaced with a mount-gated hook.
- **Four images were SVG data written to `.jpg` paths.** They passed an `image/*` content-type check, then broke Next's optimizer at request time. Now verified by magic bytes; the placeholder is emitted as a real PNG.

**Still open:**
- **Imagery** — parked per your decision. It's the visible weak spot on every screen; the offer stands if you want to revisit before submission.
- `/cart`, `/dp/*`, `/orders`, `/signin`, `/help` 404 by design — they arrive in P0 #5–9. My QA script tracks them in a separate `routesNotBuiltYet` bucket so they don't mask real faults.
- Playwright added as a devDependency; [scripts/visual-qa.mjs](scripts/visual-qa.mjs) is reusable for every later phase.
- Production server still running on `127.0.0.1:3000`. Note `localhost` resolves to IPv6 here — use the IPv4 address.

Ready for **P0 #5–6** (search results, then PDP) on your go.

timestamp: 2026-09-20T18:42:44.180Z

**P0 is complete.** The full purchase journey works end to end.

## 1. Cart functionality
`/cart` — line items with image, title, rating, variant, stock, delivery and a right-aligned price column. Quantity stepper clamped to real stock, with the trash affordance at qty 1. Save for later moves items to their own section and back. Free-shipping meter driven by `COMMERCE` config rather than hard-coded copy. Sticky summary rail, gift checkbox, Proceed to checkout, Continue shopping, Clear cart, empty-cart state, recommendations rail.

## 2. Checkout functionality
`/checkout` sits **outside** the `(shop)` route group so it renders a stripped header — no nav, no search, no departments. Three accordion steps (delivery → payment → review); completed steps collapse to a summary with a Change link.

Validation is hand-rolled with `noValidate`, so messages are ours and inline: required fields, 5-digit ZIP, 10-digit phone, **Luhn** card check, `MM/YY` expiry with a real past-date test, and a required payment selection. `Place your order` re-validates everything and jumps back to the offending step rather than trusting earlier passes.

## 3. Confirmation functionality
`/order-confirmation/[orderId]` — order id, date, delivery estimate, address, payment summary, items with quantities and unit prices, and full totals. States plainly that the order is simulated. An unknown or foreign order id gets an honest explanation rather than a blank receipt.

## 4. Persistence
`kartly.cart`, `kartly.checkout`, `kartly.orders` in localStorage. Order items are **snapshots**, so a past order keeps the price and title actually bought even if the catalogue changes. The card draft is deliberately excluded from persistence — only the last four digits survive, on the order.

## 5. End-to-end test result
**47/47 purchase checks**, from a clean browser against the production build. Your full 30-step journey plus the edge cases: quantity up/down, save and restore, delete, subtotal arithmetic, shipping meter, every validation path, place order, cart emptied, refresh, back/forward, direct `/cart` and `/checkout` with an empty cart, unknown order id, 390px and 1440px.

Regression suites still clean: **42/42** discovery, chrome suite **0 problems**.

## 6. Errors found and fixed
Zero console errors, page errors or unexpected 404s in the final run.

The one substantive thing I caught was in my **own QA**, not the app: my `money()` helper grabbed the first dollar amount in the summary rail, which is the *subtotal* — so "order total" and "subtotal" both read `1238.99` and the arithmetic was never actually checked. I rewrote it to parse each line separately. Totals now verify properly: **1238.99 + 0 shipping + 102.22 tax = 1341.21**, consistent between checkout and confirmation.

## 7. Production build
```
typecheck   exit 0
next build  clean — 126 routes (120 SSG PDPs, /cart, /checkout static,
            /s and /order-confirmation dynamic)
```

## 8. Commit
**`e566510`** — feat: complete cart checkout and order flow

Also added a **README** documenting the simulated order flow explicitly, per the brief — no payment processed, no card details collected or stored, orders are local records flagged `simulated: true`.

## 9. Remaining P1 work
10 Autocomplete · 11 Filters/sort/pagination *(basic versions already shipped — P1 adds multi-select facets with counts)* · 12 Deals/stock/delivery detail *(largely shipped)* · 13 Real homepage (hero carousel, alternating card rows) · 14 Reviews depth · 15 Responsive polish · 16 Empty states/skeletons/404. Then P2, plus `/orders` — the data is already persisted and structured for it.

timestamp: 2026-09-20T19:11:40.499Z
model: claude-opus-5

P1 #13 and #10 are done.

## Homepage sections implemented
Hero carousel · four-up card row (Top tech / Kitchen / Fashion / Toys) · **Best Sellers** rail · department grid (all 10) · **Today's Deals** rail · second card row (Sports / Beauty / Office / Pets) · **Popular in Electronics** · **Recently viewed** · **Recommended for you** · **Best Sellers in Home & Kitchen** · footer.

Everything renders through the existing `ProductCard`, `PriceBlock`, `StarRating`, `Badge` and `SectionHeader` — no one-off card implementations.

## Carousel behaviour
Four slides, arrows, dots, autoplay on a 6s interval, **pauses on hover and on focus**, ArrowLeft/Right, and a live region announcing slide changes. Autoplay is disabled entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion` — a rotating hero is a genuine accessibility hazard for anyone who needs more time to read.

Kept deliberately short (~⅓ of the fold) so the departments and product rows are visible without scrolling; a full-height hero makes a marketplace read as a landing page. No carousel library — a translated flex track and an interval.

Product rails scroll natively (touch, trackpad and keyboard work for free); the arrows are an addition that pages by a viewport and **hide themselves at each end**, so they're never dead controls.

## Autocomplete behaviour
Suggestions come from a local `/api/suggest` route reading the same static catalogue — a route rather than a client-side index, because the header is on every page and shipping a 120-product index into every bundle for a dropdown most visits never open is a poor trade. Debounced 120ms, in-flight requests aborted, results cached per term.

Query, department and product suggestions; matched prefix stays regular and the completion goes **bold**; scope shown as "in Computers"; product rows carry a thumbnail and price. The scrim is portalled to `body` at z-40 so the z-50 header stays lit while the page recedes.

The form is still a plain GET form to `/s`, so **search works with JavaScript off**.

## Keyboard accessibility
Full combobox pattern — the input keeps focus and owns `aria-activedescendant` while the listbox owns the options, so a screen reader announces the highlighted suggestion without focus moving. `ArrowDown` / `ArrowUp` (wrapping) / `Home` / `End` / `Enter` / `Escape`, plus click and click-outside. No focus trap.

## Browser QA result
**42/42** homepage + autocomplete checks, and no regressions: **42/42** discovery, **47/47** purchase, chrome suite clean. **173 checks, 0 problems.** No dead homepage links (I resolve every one), no broken images, no horizontal overflow at 390 or 1440.

## Bug found and fixed
The QA surfaced a real relevance bug, not just a failing assertion. Typing "laptop" suggested a **USB cable** and a **wall charger** above every actual laptop — both titles end "…Laptop and Phone Compatible", and I was ranking purely by review count. Suggestions are now scored by match quality (a product's own category term beats a word buried in a compatibility clause), with popularity only breaking ties. `laptop` now returns Laptop Stand → Stratus 14 Laptop → Forge 16 Gaming Laptop.

## Production build
```
typecheck   exit 0
next build  clean — /api/suggest added, 120 SSG PDPs intact
```

11. Sort works together with filters.
12. Pagination works with filters and sorting.

Example URL structure:

/s?q=laptop&brand=Aureon&brand=Nordvik&rating=4&price=500-1000

Use the actual catalog rather than hard-coded result counts.

Do not create fake facet counts.

Counts should be derived from the currently applicable result set according to the chosen filtering model.

On desktop:

- filters should remain visually similar to the Amazon-style left rail in /recon/filter.png

- Rating

- previous

- next
- disabled states

Do not over-engineer pagination.

timestamp: 2026-09-20T19:46:25.590Z
model: claude-opus-5

**P1 complete.**

## 1. Filters
Multi-select facets — department, brand, price bracket, rating, availability, deals, plus **category-specific spec facets** (RAM, Processor, Storage appear under Computers, only once the result set is narrow enough for the keys to be shared).

Values within a facet are **OR'd**, different facets **AND'd**. Counts are computed with every *other* facet applied but not the facet itself, so you see what adding another brand would return — verified: a facet claiming `(2)` yields exactly 2 results. Options that would return zero are hidden. Removable chips per filter, plus clear-all. All state in the URL: `/s?q=laptop&brand=Kestrel&brand=Meridia&rating=4&sort=price-asc`.

The rail is **one DOM instance repositioned by CSS** — static left rail on desktop, slide-in drawer on mobile. Rendering it twice behind media queries would duplicate every filter and its `aria-pressed` state in the accessibility tree.

## 2. Reviews
Sorting (top/recent/highest/lowest), helpful toggle with live count, see-all expansion, theme chips, verified badges. **Clicking a histogram row filters the list to that star rating.** Still the one generated dataset — histogram, headline rating and summary all read from the same numbers (verified: percentages sum to 99%, matching the 4.6 headline).

## 3. Responsive issues fixed
Swept **1440 / 1280 / 1024 / 768 / 480 / 390 / 375** across home, search, PDP, cart, checkout, orders and 404 — 49 route×width combinations. **No horizontal overflow anywhere**, checked both by `scrollWidth` and by finding any element extending past the viewport.

## 4. States
Branded 404 (search box, all 10 departments, real products — never a dead end) · search skeleton mirroring the real rail-plus-rows layout · empty cart · empty saved-for-later · no results (different copy when filters are the cause) · empty query · checkout with empty cart · unknown product · unknown order. No artificial delays added.

## 5. /orders
Order list with status, total, item count, thumbnails, delivery estimate, expandable line items, "View order details" and "Buy it again". Reachable from the header's Returns & Orders.

## 6–8. QA, build, errors
```
P1         54/54        typecheck  exit 0
homepage   42/42        next build clean — 126 routes
discovery  42/42
purchase   47/47        console errors  0
chrome     clean        page errors     0
────────────────         unexpected 404s 0
239 checks, 0 problems   overflow        0
```

**Regression I introduced and fixed:** adding a loading skeleton to the PDP made Next commit a **200 status** before the segment could call `notFound()`, so unknown product ids silently stopped returning 404. The skeleton was pointless there anyway — all 120 PDPs are statically generated — so I removed it. The search skeleton stays, because `/s` genuinely renders on demand.

## 9. Imagery — the report you asked for

**Most damaging, in order:**
1. **Homepage department tiles (10 assets).** Largest images on the first screen. 2 of 10 are clearly wrong — Home & Kitchen shows `20120106-OC-AMW-0078`, Office Products shows `3x4 Poster`.
2. **Card-row tiles (~32 tiles across 8 cards).** Second thing you see; a bear illustrating a frying pan sits here.
3. **Hero slides (4).** Currently reuse department heroes, so fixing #1 fixes these.
4. Search-result and PDP images — less damaging, because dense text carries the context.

**Scale:** ~18% of the 120 (≈22 assets) are off-topic by a title-match heuristic, worst in Home & Kitchen, Beauty and Office (4/12 each). The true visual mismatch is higher, since even on-topic photos are documentary rather than studio shots.

**Would targeted replacement help?** Yes, materially and cheaply. Replacing roughly **30 assets** — the 10 department heroes plus the ~20 distinct card-row tiles — would fix the entire first impression, which is where the imagery costs most. That's a fraction of a full re-library and I'd expect it to be the single highest-value hour left. Not done, per your instruction.

## 10. Commit
**`4f10153`** — feat: complete P1 marketplace polish

## 11. Remaining
P2 (not started): save-for-later depth, department mega-drawer, Frequently Bought Together, mock sign-in. Plus the two still-unbuilt routes `/signin` and `/help`, and **deployment**, still deferred — `next build` is green and Vercel needs no config for this stack.

The HOME suite is still running; I'll continue when the watcher reports. Meanwhile the imagery work itself is done and verified — here's where it stands.

**19 images replaced**, all from Openverse, downloaded locally into `/public/products`, no runtime dependency on any external host.

**All 10 department tiles now read correctly:**

| Department | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Home & Kitchen | fridge interior | chef knife |
| Clothing & Accessories | art-deco building | wristwatch |
| Beauty & Personal Care | tropical island | cosmetics display |
| Toys & Games | empty studio room | crayons |
| Books | classical painting | actual books |
| Office Products | cardboard boxes | coloured pencils |

Electronics (USB cable), Computers (USB hub), Sports (hiker) and Pet Supplies (dog at fountain) were already correct and were left alone.

**Card rows also fixed:** real earbuds, a headlamp being worn, jigsaw pieces, open book pages, cosmetic bottles, a sneaker, a loudspeaker, a circuit board.

Two things worth flagging from the process:

**I nearly shipped something worse.** The first pass — CC0-only — returned anime fan art for "earphones" and a *Lorem ipsum* graphic for "art supplies". I checked each result on a contact sheet rather than trusting filenames, caught it, and widened those 12 slots to CC-BY. That's within your brief ("*prefer* CC0 where possible"), and ATTRIBUTION.md now correctly states **108 public-domain / 12 attribution-required**, each with creator, licence link and source page. The old header claiming "no attribution requirement" would have been false.

**My first verification was reading stale output.** Replacing files at the same paths doesn't bust Next's image optimizer cache, so the homepage was still serving the old bear. I cleared `.next` and re-verified — worth knowing for the eventual deploy, though Vercel builds fresh so it won't bite there.

**Verified at 1440 / 1024 / 390:** 112 images, **0 broken**, no horizontal overflow, all 4 hero slides load, 0 console/page errors.

**Remaining imperfect imagery** (all deeper than the first screen, none in a department tile): the bedding-linen and frying-pan tiles in Home & Kitchen are food/fabric rather than the product; the cooler bag and pet feeder are ambiguous; the mechanical keyboard has a glue stick in shot. I judged these not worth further time against your box.

I have **not committed yet** — I'll do that once the remaining suites confirm no regressions, then report the hash.

Electronics, Computers, Sports and Pet Supplies tiles were already relevant and left untouched, per your "if it's reasonably relevant, keep it".

## Remaining obviously incorrect imagery
None in a department tile or hero. Deeper in the card rows: the Home & Kitchen bedding-linen and frying-pan tiles show fabric and a bowl of food rather than the product; the cooler bag and pet feeder are ambiguous; the mechanical keyboard has a glue stick in shot. All are plausible-but-imprecise rather than jarring, and I judged them not worth more time against your box.

## Two things you should know

**The CC0-only pass produced results worse than what they replaced** — anime fan art for "earphones", a *Lorem ipsum* graphic for "art supplies". I caught these on the contact sheet and didn't ship them. Twelve high-visibility slots now use CC-BY/CC-BY-SA, which is legally fine to redistribute publicly with credit; 108 of 120 remain CC0/public domain. ATTRIBUTION.md previously asserted the whole library carried no attribution requirement — that would now have been **false**, so it's regenerated with a licence breakdown and per-image creator, licence link and source page.

**My first verification was reading stale images.** Replacing a file at the same path doesn't invalidate Next's image optimizer cache, so the homepage was still serving the old bear. Cleared `.next` and re-verified. Vercel builds fresh, so this only affects local checks — but it's why the check is worth repeating after any future image swap.

## QA result
**185 checks, 0 problems** — chrome clean, home 42/42, discovery 42/42, purchase 47/47, P1 54/54.

Homepage verified at 1440 / 1024 / 390: **112 images, 0 broken**, no horizontal overflow, all 4 hero slides load, 0 console/page errors.

Homepage refinement complete.

## Major visual improvements

**Hero — the main change.** Measured against the recon (whose promo banner is ≈218px at a 1520px viewport), ours was **300px with a 38px headline** — a band that tall reads as a landing page and pushed the departments below the fold. Now **240px** with a **31px** headline, tightened eyebrow and sub, and enlarged art that fills the shorter band rather than floating in it. The first card row *and* Best Sellers now appear above the fold.

**Consistent image treatment.** Card-row and department tiles are editorial — the label names the thing, the picture sets the mood — so they now crop to fill. Letterboxing mixed-ratio photos inside small squares left ragged white gaps and made two adjacent grids read as unfinished. Product imagery elsewhere stays `object-contain`, because there the whole item must be visible.

**Density.** Rail cards 180 → 164px (more product per row at the same text size), rail padding trimmed, footer pulled closer. **Page height 5158 → 4938px with identical content.**

**Deliberately left alone** because they already matched the reference: 16px section gaps, card padding, 8px radius, 21px section headings, page background. No gradients, animations or glassmorphism added.

## Responsive improvements
Hero scales 240 / 210 / 190 with headline 31 / 26 / 21. At 768 the card row reflows to 2-up; at 390 rails stay swipeable and tiles go 2-up. Section gaps stay a uniform 16px at every width, no horizontal overflow at any of the six.

**Two bugs my own resize introduced, both caught and fixed:**
1. Shortening the band put the **indicator dots on top of the CTA** at 768 and 390.
2. The **side arrows overlapped the CTA horizontally** at 375/390 — a tap near the button edge would hit the arrow. Both now have measured clearance everywhere (dots ≥11px, arrows ≥8px).

I also found a flaw in my own measurement: the probe was reading the *first* slide, which the track translates off-screen, producing nonsense like `-2650px` clearance. It now selects the slide actually inside the hero box — without that fix I'd have "verified" a layout I hadn't measured.

## QA result
**51/51** homepage checks at all six widths — hero next/previous/dots, autoplay, pause-on-hover, rail scrolling, department navigation, product cards, mobile dots, and **all 80 homepage links resolving**.

Full regression clean: chrome **0 problems**, home **42/42**, discovery **42/42**, purchase **47/47**, P1 **54/54**. Zero console errors, zero page errors, zero broken images.

While those run — the substantive change here, beyond building the drawer, was fixing a real defect your brief anticipated.

**Before:** `MobileMenu` was rendered twice (once by the mobile hamburger, once by the desktop "All"), so every page shipped **2 `role="dialog"` elements and 2 scrims**. Screen-reader users would encounter two identical department menus, one permanently hidden.

**Now:** one `DepartmentDrawer`, rendered once by `SiteHeader`, driven by two triggers through a small shared store. Verified: `exactly one dialog in the DOM` at all four widths.

The drawer content is entirely catalogue-derived — 10 departments with real product counts, expanding to the brands actually stocked in that department. **All 16 links resolve** (I deliberately left out `/signin` and `/help`, which don't exist, rather than shipping links to nowhere).

One build break worth noting: the drawer reads `useSearchParams` to highlight the active department, which without a Suspense boundary opted **all 120 PDPs out of static rendering and failed the build**. Wrapped it, same as the search bar.

[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=48 session=c4110ea9]
timestamp: 2026-09-20T22:23:56.357Z
model: claude-opus-5

While those run, the design decisions worth calling out:

**No ML, no behavioural data** — a hand-written map of which product *type* complements which, keyed on the catalogue's own search terms. Verified across all 120 products: **every one gets a 4-item bundle, zero empties, zero duplicates**, and no product ever suggests another of its own kind. Pairings read correctly — laptop → mouse/stand/hub, tent → sleeping bag/head torch/cooler, cookbook → chef knife/cutting board/cookware.

**Cart logic isn't duplicated** — the button loops over the selection and calls the existing `useCart().add`, the same path every other add-to-cart uses. That's why the cart subtotal matched the bundle total exactly in QA ($1,238.99 both sides).

**It doesn't dominate** — measured at **4% of page height**, sitting between the buy box and the specifications.

There's a graceful-degradation detail: `getBundle` returns an empty list rather than filler if it can't find at least two in-stock complements, and the component renders nothing in that case. Today that never triggers, but it means a future catalogue change can't produce a one-item "bundle".

timestamp: 2026-09-20T23:11:18.442Z
model: claude-opus-5

Frequently Bought Together is done.

## What was built
A compact PDP section — thumbnail strip with `+` separators, running total, and a checkbox list — sitting between the buy box and the specifications at **4% of page height**, so it's noticeable without competing with the buy box.

Complements are **deterministic, not learned**: `lib/bundles.ts` maps product *type* to complementary types using the catalogue's own search terms. Verified across the whole catalogue — **all 120 products yield a 4-item bundle, zero duplicates, and no product ever suggests another of its own kind**:

```
laptop computer  -> computer mouse, laptop stand, usb hub
camping tent     -> sleeping bag, headlamp torch, cooler bag
cookbook         -> chef knife, cutting board, cookware set
cat tree         -> cat litter mat, pet feeder, pet grooming brush
```

Cart logic isn't duplicated — the button loops the selection through the existing `useCart().add`, which is why the cart subtotal matched the bundle total exactly ($1,238.99 both sides).

## QA
**23/23 bundle checks** — selection, deselection, combined-total arithmetic, disabled empty state, dimmed deselected thumbnails, header cart count, cart contents, cart subtotal, mobile layout and mobile add.

**A real regression, found and fixed.** The P1 sweep caught **horizontal overflow on the PDP at exactly 1024px**: the `lg:` three-column layout squeezed the checkbox list to ~200px, where a long title plus a `whitespace-nowrap` price pushed the page 19px past the viewport. Moved the row layout to `xl` and let the label wrap. Re-checked at 1440/1280/1024/768/480/390/375 — **no overflow at any width**, P1 back to 54/54.

Full regression clean: chrome 0 problems · discovery 42/42 · purchase 47/47 · P1 54/54 · drawer 65/65 · home 42/42.

`typecheck` exit 0 · `next build` clean.

## One correction
In my previous step I reported the drawer suite as **53/53**. The real figure is **65/65** — I counted visible lines in a truncated terminal tail rather than reading the summary line. All checks passed either way, but commit `58784c4`'s message carries the same wrong number.

## Commit
**`a5102e6`** — feat: add frequently bought together

Stopping here. Not deployed.

[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=69 session=c4110ea9]
timestamp: 2026-09-21T03:38:30.658Z
model: claude-opus-5

Simulated sign-in is complete.

## What was built
`/signin` in its own `(auth)` route group with minimal chrome — wordmark, form, legal footer, no nav or search, for the same reason checkout is stripped.

**Nothing is authenticated.** No server, no session, no token. The password is validated for shape and then **discarded** — never stored, never persisted, never leaves the browser. Only a display name and the typed identifier go to localStorage, so the header has something to greet and sign-out has something to clear. QA asserts this directly: `no password is stored` passes against the actual stored object.

- Email-or-phone + password with hand-rolled inline validation (`noValidate`), matching checkout
- **Create-account mode** adds a name field rather than being a dead link
- **Forgot password** explains the demo instead of going nowhere
- `?next=` returns you where you started — signing in from `/orders` lands back on `/orders`
- Header swaps to "Hello, Farhan" with a menu (Your Orders / Your Cart / Sign out); the drawer greeting and account row follow the same state

**Signing in is never required** — verified explicitly in a clean signed-out context: search returns results, add-to-cart works, checkout is reachable.

## QA
**36/36 sign-in checks** — direct navigation, empty submit, invalid email, short password, short phone, forgot-password, create-account mode, successful sign-in, stored contents, absence of any stored password, refresh persistence, account menu, sign out, return-path, mobile layout and mobile sign-in.

**Full regression clean:** chrome 0 problems · drawer 65/65 · discovery 42/42 · purchase 47/47 · P1 54/54 · bundle 23/23 · home 42/42.

`routesNotBuiltYet` is now down to **just `/help`** — `/signin` is no longer a dead link.

## Three things worth flagging
1. **Shell mangling nearly shipped broken validation.** Writing the regexes through a shell heredoc turned `\D` into `D` and `\s` into `s`, which would have made the phone check strip the letter "D" and the email check accept malformed addresses. Caught it by testing the regexes as written in the file, then fixed them directly.
2. **Both test failures were mine, not the app's.** The instructive one: I looked for `getByRole("button", { name: "Sign out" })`, but inside `role="menu"` that control is correctly a `menuitem` — the component had the right semantics and my assertion had the wrong expectation.
3. **Four PDP prerender timeouts** in one build turned out to be CPU contention from two stray node processes, not a code fault — a clean build has none. Worth knowing rather than assuming.

## Commit
**`107f263`** — feat: add simulated sign-in

Stopping here. Not deployed.

timestamp: 2026-09-21T04:06:42.730Z
model: claude-opus-5

Help centre complete.

## What was built
`/help` — the last route the app linked to but didn't have. Heading with a plain GET search form, six topics (**Your Orders, Delivery, Returns & Refunds, Payments & Pricing, Your Account, About this demo**), and **24 articles**.

Every article is a native `<details>` element, so the whole page works **with no JavaScript** and is keyboard accessible without a line of custom key handling — verified: summaries take focus and Enter toggles both ways.

## Honest content, no fake functionality
Every answer describes how the application actually behaves — where state lives, that checkout is simulated and nothing is charged, that orders are per-browser, which features are deliberately absent, and that some product photography is only loosely related to the item it illustrates.

**There is no contact form.** A form that went nowhere would be exactly the fake functionality your brief rules out, so the CTA says plainly there's no support team and points to Your Orders and the catalogue instead.

## Every dead end now resolves
The entry points that previously all landed nowhere — Customer Service, Registry, Gift Cards and Sell in the subnav, the footer columns, and Conditions of Use / Privacy Notice in all three footers — now reach content that answers them. Registry, gift cards and selling get an explicit "not part of this demo, and here's why" rather than silence.

## QA
**26/26 help checks** — direct navigation, topic coverage, collapsed-then-expandable articles, keyboard focus and Enter toggling, anchor jumps, search results with counts, the no-result recovery state, empty query, link integrity, subnav and footer entry points, and no overflow at 1440/1024/768/390/375.

**Full regression clean:** chrome 0 problems · sign-in 36/36 · drawer 65/65 · discovery 42/42 · purchase 47/47 · P1 54/54 · bundle 23/23 · home 42/42.

Notably, the chrome suite now reports **`routesNotBuiltYet: (none)`** — for the first time, every route the application links to exists.

`typecheck` exit 0 · `next build` clean.

Step 7 is committed as `276dc64`. Stopping here as instructed.

**What the audit found and fixed**

Six style families had drifted apart. `scripts/audit-design.mjs` walks nine routes collecting computed styles and tallies distinct values, which is what turned "it feels slightly off" into a list:

- **Cards** — 20px vs 16px padding; now 26 of 31 card containers share `card p-4 sm:p-5`. The five outliers are genuinely different objects (empty states, hero panels).
- **Headings** — six different page-`H1` treatments collapsed to a role-based scale: 28/400 page titles, 24/400 product and empty-state titles, 21 and 18 section heads, 16 sub-heads, 15 rail heads. The stray 19px and 17px sizes are gone.
- **Form controls** — five height/border combinations down to two families (33px dropdowns, 34px form fields) on one border grey.
- **Hero CTA** — was bespoke; now routes through `ButtonLink`, so every yellow call to action shares one definition of colour, border, shadow, hover and focus.

**Two real defects the responsive sweep caught**

The sweep at 1440/1280/1024/768/480/390/375 found more than layout drift:

1. [FilterShell.tsx:72](components/search/FilterShell.tsx#L72) — the mobile filter panel was parked off-canvas with a transform only. A transform doesn't remove anything from the tab order or the accessibility tree, so on a phone a keyboard user tabbing past the results toolbar fell into ~29 filter controls they couldn't see. It now transitions `visibility` alongside `transform`; because `visibility` flips discretely at the *end* of a transition, this hides the panel without losing the slide-out animation, and it stays one instance serving both the mobile drawer and the desktop rail.
2. [HeroCarousel.tsx:161](components/home/HeroCarousel.tsx#L161) — the carousel indicators were 7×7px buttons; the tap target *was* the dot. The dot stays small, the button around it is now 24px.

Worth noting: my first sweep reported ~40 failures, and nearly all were the probe's fault — it flagged correctly-parked off-canvas panels as escaping the viewport. I fixed the probe to respect ancestor `visibility` and `aria-hidden` rather than "fixing" the app. Only the two items above were real.

**Verification against the production build** — 189/189 responsive checks, and nine QA suites green: visual-qa (no problems, no unbuilt routes), home 42/42, discovery 42/42, purchase 47/47, p1 54/54, drawer 65/65, fbt 23/23, signin 36/36, help 26/26. Not deployed. `.agent-logs/` committed alongside, unmodified.

Two housekeeping notes: the production server is still running on `127.0.0.1:3000` if you want to look. And several claude.ai connectors (Slack, Linear, Figma and others) reported they need authorization — they're unusable until you authorize them in your claude.ai connector settings, though nothing in this work needed them.

Done — committed as `fb84e95`.

**What it was.** Not just irrelevant: the merino t-shirt page was illustrated with a paparazzi beach candid of an identifiable celebrity, in a state of undress. Titled "Kim Kardashian – Candids During Wet T-Shirt Photoshoot In Mexico."

**How it got there.** It came through the normal pipeline. The query `"t-shirt clothing"` ranked it above every photo of an actual shirt, and it carried a Public Domain Mark, so the CC0/PDM licence filter passed it. A licence filter tells you what you may reuse, never what is appropriate to reuse — and a public-domain mark on a paparazzi frame of a living person is dubious on its own terms.

**The replacement** is a CC0 stack of folded jersey tees from rawpixel: no people, no third-party branding, no attribution obligation. 600px wide, matching the rest of the set. It renders on the PDP gallery, the thumbnails and the frequently-bought-together row.

**Two pipeline changes, because my first attempt failed.** I added a query override to `"folded t-shirt"` and re-ran — and it picked a 1995 University of Michigan conference tee plastered with someone else's logo. The pool contains both good and bad results and the pipeline's `size=medium` filter had excluded the clean shots I'd verified. So [fetch-images.mjs:196](scripts/fetch-images.mjs#L196) now has a `PINNED` map that fetches a specific verified Openverse id before falling back to the ranked pool. Ranking is not review; pinning is what makes an eyeball check survive a re-run.

ATTRIBUTION.md regenerated itself, dropping the celebrity credit. Build clean, 0/112 broken images, qa-home 42/42, qa-fbt 23/23.

**Two things you should decide on.**

1. **The old image is still in git history.** Commit `fb84e95` removes it from the working tree, but the blob remains reachable in earlier commits — so it would ship with the repo when you make it public. If you want it genuinely gone, that needs a history rewrite (`git filter-repo`) or a fresh initial commit. Say the word and I'll do it; I haven't, because rewriting history is destructive and touches your `.agent-logs/` commits too.

2. **I scanned the other 119 provenance records** for the same defect class. No explicit red flags. Seven titles mention people, and all read benign from the metadata (a parka listing, a scarf, playing cards, a woman walking a dog). But that scan reads *titles*, not pixels — which is precisely the mistake that let this one through. `scripts/contact-sheet.mjs` builds a visual sheet of all 120 if you want an actual eyes-on pass before the repo goes public. I'd recommend it.

The production server is still up on `127.0.0.1:3000`.

Fixed, committed as `1831624`.

**What was actually happening.** The file on disk was already correct — I'd verified the bytes, and the server was serving the folded-tees photo. What you were seeing was a cached copy in your browser.

Next serves optimized images with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=14400` keyed on the source path, and that path hadn't changed, because the image filename follows the product slug. Nothing in the request told your browser that the photograph behind that URL was now a different one, so it kept serving the old bytes from its own cache for up to four hours. A hard refresh would have cleared it — but that's a fix you'd have to apply, and so would every other visitor, and so would Vercel's image cache once you deploy.

**So I changed the URL instead.** [fetch-images.mjs:196](scripts/fetch-images.mjs#L196) now has an `IMAGE_FILE_OVERRIDES` map that lets a product's image filename differ from its slug, and `fashion-03` resolves to `...-fashion-03-v2.jpg`. The old path is now genuinely gone: 404 as a static file, 400 through the optimizer. No cache anywhere can produce those bytes, because nothing will ask for that URL again. The product slug and PDP URL are unchanged.

**Verified across every surface you'd see it on** — homepage, Today's Deals, the fashion listing and the PDP: zero references to the old filename, one to the new. qa-home 42/42 with 0/112 broken images, qa-fbt 23/23, qa-discovery 42/42. The deals rail now shows the folded tees.

You should see it on a normal reload now — no hard refresh needed, since the page HTML points at a URL your browser has never seen.

One correction to something I implied last time: I said the image was gone once the file was replaced. That was true of the repository but not of what people were actually being served, and you were right to push back.

The history point from before still stands and is now more relevant, since there are two dead paths in it: the old blob remains reachable in earlier commits, so it would ship when the repo goes public. That needs a history rewrite to remove properly — say the word and I'll do it.

FINAL POLISH — MODERN AMAZON-INSPIRED UX + HEADER INTERACTION CONSISTENCY

We are now at the final refinement stage before the final evaluator/red-team audit.

P0 and P1 are complete and the core shopping experience is already working.

Do NOT treat this as a new feature-development phase.

The objective is to make Kartly feel like a polished, modern, production-quality marketplace rather than a static recreation of an older Amazon UI.

IMPORTANT PRODUCT DIRECTION

I reviewed the current Amazon experience and noticed that Amazon's homepage has evolved significantly from the older reference screenshots we initially used.

Therefore:

DO NOT blindly freeze Kartly to the older Amazon screenshots.

Instead:

- preserve the recognizable marketplace information architecture
- preserve the dense, commerce-oriented layout
- preserve strong product discovery
- improve the UX where the newer Amazon-style patterns are clearly better
- maintain Kartly's own branding
- do not copy Amazon's logo, proprietary branding, or exact visual identity
- do not turn Kartly into a generic SaaS landing page

The goal is:

"Amazon-inspired marketplace UX, but polished and modernized for Kartly."

==================================================
1. HEADER + CART INTERACTION CONSISTENCY
==================================================

This is the highest-priority refinement.

I noticed several small inconsistencies around the header cart interaction.

Review the complete interaction from:

Add to Cart
→ cart count update
→ button feedback
→ cart drawer opening
→ drawer content update
→ subtotal update
→ Go to Cart

Make the entire sequence feel intentional and consistent.

EXPECTED BEHAVIOR:

When the user clicks "Add to cart":

1. The action should have a subtle, minimal visual confirmation.

Examples of acceptable feedback:
- very small button state change
- brief pressed/active state
- subtle scale/opacity transition
- cart icon/count update
- brief visual confirmation near the cart

Do NOT use:
- large animations
- bouncing elements
- flashy effects
- excessive motion
- distracting transitions

The interaction should feel similar to a polished large-scale e-commerce site.

==================================================
2. CART DRAWER
==================================================

Review the mini-cart/cart drawer behavior.

The drawer should:

- open consistently after the intended Add to Cart interaction
- animate smoothly but minimally
- not feel abrupt
- show the newly added item immediately
- update quantity correctly
- update subtotal correctly
- update the cart badge correctly
- keep the drawer visually aligned with the header
- provide a clear "Go to Cart" action
- close predictably
- not interfere with the rest of the page

Check all of these states:

A. Add first product
B. Add another product
C. Increase quantity
D. Decrease quantity
E. Remove item
F. Navigate to cart
G. Return to shopping and add another item
H. Refresh page and verify persisted cart state

Do not redesign the entire cart system.

Use the existing cart architecture.

Fix only the interaction inconsistencies.

==================================================
3. HEADER VISUAL CONSISTENCY
==================================================

Review the complete header as one unified component.

Pay particular attention to:

- logo/wordmark alignment
- delivery/location area
- search bar
- search category selector
- search button
- language/currency area
- account area
- returns/orders
- cart icon
- cart badge
- secondary navigation row

The header should feel like one coherent system.

Check:

- vertical alignment
- spacing
- typography
- icon sizes
- hover states
- active/pressed states
- border treatment
- transitions
- cart badge positioning
- responsive behavior

Do not make the header unnecessarily taller.

Do not introduce large rounded SaaS-style containers.

==================================================
4. CURRENCY / LANGUAGE DISPLAY
==================================================

There is currently some inconsistency in how currency/language information is displayed.

Review the existing implementation and make it consistent across:

- header
- product cards
- PDP
- cart
- checkout
- cart drawer
- order confirmation
- orders page

Use one coherent currency presentation system.

The important requirement is consistency rather than adding a complex currency-conversion system.

Do NOT introduce real currency conversion or external APIs.

This remains a simulated/local marketplace.

If the header currently shows language/currency information, make its typography, spacing, iconography, and dropdown behavior consistent with the rest of the application.

==================================================
5. HOMEPAGE — MODERNIZE THE UX, DON'T CLONE OLD AMAZON
==================================================

I also reviewed a newer Amazon homepage.

The newer layout demonstrates a more modern approach:

- strong visual category discovery
- dense product/category modules
- recommendation-driven sections
- more varied content modules
- strong visual hierarchy
- commerce-oriented cards
- multiple horizontal discovery areas
- less dependence on a single giant hero
- more useful content above and below the fold

Use these principles as UX inspiration.

Do NOT attempt to reproduce Amazon's exact homepage.

Kartly should remain its own product.

The current homepage already has:

- hero
- department/category discovery
- product carousels
- category cards
- recommendations
- footer

Refine the composition so that it feels intentional and modern.

Prioritize:

1. Above-the-fold hierarchy
2. Hero/category discovery
3. Department discovery
4. Product recommendation rows
5. Category merchandising modules
6. Consistent card proportions
7. Section spacing
8. Mobile behavior
9. Visual rhythm between sections

The homepage should feel dense enough for an e-commerce marketplace, but not visually chaotic.

==================================================
6. DO NOT OVER-DESIGN
==================================================

Avoid the following:

- excessive rounded cards
- excessive shadows
- glassmorphism
- gradients everywhere
- huge whitespace
- giant SaaS-style hero sections
- oversized typography
- unnecessary animations
- excessive hover effects
- excessive borders
- redesigning working components simply for novelty

Kartly should feel like a serious marketplace.

Think:

dense + useful + visual + fast + trustworthy.

Not:

marketing landing page.

==================================================
7. RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOR
==================================================

Make sure the refinement does not break the existing responsive system.

Pay particular attention to:

- 1440px desktop
- 1024px tablet/small desktop
- 390px mobile

At mobile widths:

- header must remain usable
- cart interaction must remain clear
- drawer must fit the viewport
- no horizontal overflow
- homepage modules should remain readable
- buttons should remain tappable
- product cards should not become cramped

Do not redesign the responsive architecture.

Only fix issues introduced or discovered during this refinement.

==================================================
8. REUSE EXISTING DESIGN SYSTEM
==================================================

Before creating new CSS/components, inspect the existing primitives and reuse them.

Prefer existing:

- Button
- PriceBlock
- StarRating
- Badge
- ProductCard
- SectionHeader
- QuantityStepper
- existing layout/container utilities

If a small shared improvement is needed, make it at the primitive level rather than adding one-off styles.

The goal is to reduce visual inconsistencies across the application.

==================================================
9. KEEP KARTLY'S IDENTITY
==================================================

Do not replace the Kartly brand with Amazon branding.

Do not add the Amazon logo.

Do not copy proprietary Amazon assets.

The visual language can be inspired by large e-commerce marketplaces, but Kartly must remain clearly its own product.

==================================================
10. SCOPE CONTROL
==================================================

This is NOT a request to:

- add authentication
- add a payment gateway
- add backend infrastructure
- add new database systems
- redesign the entire catalog
- rebuild the cart
- rewrite routing
- add unrelated features
- deploy to Vercel

Only refine the existing experience.

If something already works well, leave it alone.

==================================================
11. MINIMAL QA ONLY
==================================================

Do NOT spend a large amount of time on a full regression QA pass.

After implementation, perform only a lightweight smoke check:

- production build succeeds
- homepage loads
- add-to-cart works
- cart drawer opens and updates
- cart count updates
- subtotal updates
- no obvious console error caused by these changes
- check desktop around 1440px
- check mobile around 390px

Do NOT rerun the entire previous 200+ check QA suite unless a real regression is discovered.

Do NOT spend the task on exhaustive testing.

==================================================
12. IMPLEMENTATION PROCESS
==================================================

Before changing code:

1. Inspect the current header/cart implementation.
2. Inspect the current homepage implementation.
3. Identify the smallest set of changes needed.
4. Reuse existing components and design tokens.

Then implement the refinements.

Keep changes focused.

==================================================

==================================================

When finished, the experience should communicate:

"modern, polished e-commerce marketplace"

rather than:

The important distinction is:

NOT AMAZON'S EXACT CURRENT DESIGN.

Kartly should combine:

Do not deploy to Vercel.
