timestamp: 2026-09-21T07:50:57.017Z
model: claude-opus-5

Committed as `9908b78`.

## Header / cart interaction issues fixed

**There was no mini-cart.** The brief asked me to review drawer behaviour, but nothing opened on add — the badge counted up, the button flashed, and that was it. I built one: a single instance rendered by `SiteHeader`, opened through a shared store exactly as the department drawer works, so there's one dialog in the accessibility tree rather than one per button. It reads the same cart store and the same `resolveLines`/`computeTotals` as `/cart`, which is what stops the two from disagreeing about a subtotal.

**The drawer opens on deliberate adds only** — buy box and frequently-bought-together — not on a quick add from a listing grid. I originally wired it everywhere, and testing showed why that's wrong: a shopper adding three items from a results page has to dismiss a modal three times. Listing quick-adds keep their inline confirmation plus the badge nudge.

**The same action confirmed itself two ways**: 1400ms and "Added" from a listing, 1600ms and "Added to cart" from the buy box. One timing, one label, one constant now. The badge gets a 10% scale for 220ms when the count rises, under `motion-safe`.

**The hover treatment had three copies** — a literal in [SiteHeader.tsx](components/chrome/SiteHeader.tsx), a second in [AccountArea.tsx](components/chrome/AccountArea.tsx), and a third in [CartButton.tsx](components/chrome/CartButton.tsx) that had lost its `transition-colors`, so the cart snapped on hover while everything beside it faded. One exported definition, now with a focus-visible state too.

## Homepage UX refinements

Composition only — no new modules, components or styles. Department discovery moved from four modules down to directly under the hero, with the deals rail up behind it, so the page leads with categories instead of leaning on the carousel. Rails and card rows now alternate rather than clustering. Everything else was left alone.

## Currency / language — yes, normalized

The symbol and locale were written inline in `splitPrice` and again in `formatPrice`, while `COMMERCE.currency` was defined and never used. All three now derive from one `CURRENCY` definition. Free-shipping copy on the homepage and in the help centre derives from `COMMERCE` instead of restating 35 and 5.99 by hand.

The `EN` control was a button with a chevron and no menu behind it — the only thing in the header that looked interactive and did nothing. It now opens a real popover on the account menu's pattern. It deliberately does not offer currencies that aren't there: Kartly prices in one currency, so the menu says so rather than shipping a switcher that changes no prices.

One thing I had to correct mid-task: routing copy through `formatPrice` turned "free delivery over $35" into "over $35.00", which reads like an invoice. Added `formatPriceShort` for prose so totals keep their decimals and sentences don't.

## Smoke test result

49/49 at 1440 and 390 — add, drawer opens, added-item marker, badge, quantity up and down with subtotal tracking both ways, remove, Escape, Go to Cart, re-add, reload persistence, consecutive quick adds, no console errors, no horizontal overflow, drawer fits the viewport.

I went past the minimal check in one respect, and I think correctly: **three real regressions turned up**, so I confirmed the suites that touch add-to-cart or the header rather than assuming. Purchase 48/48, discovery 42/42, drawer 69/69, home 42/42, p1 54/54, fbt 23/23, signin 36/36, visual-qa no problems.

Of those three: the listing-grid interception was a genuine product defect, fixed in the product. The two buy-box cases are correct modal behaviour — the suites encoded the old flow, so they now dismiss the drawer and say why in a comment. `qa-drawer` asserted a single `[role="dialog"]` app-wide; that assertion was about a drawer not being duplicated across breakpoints, so it's now scoped per drawer and covers both.

## Build

Clean. Commit `9908b78`.

## Left unchanged, deliberately

- **Department tile imagery.** Several tiles are weak for their category (a USB cable for Electronics), and the Toys tile carries a visible stock-photo watermark advertising another site. Highly visible on the homepage and worth fixing, but it needs the image-sourcing pipeline, which is a different task than interaction polish.
- **The department drawer's closed links stay tabbable.** It uses `aria-hidden` when closed, which hides it from screen readers but not from Tab — the same defect I fixed in the filter panel earlier. I gave the new mini-cart the correct treatment but didn't touch the existing drawer, since it's working behaviour outside this brief's scope.
- **The mini-cart costs ~13KB gzipped on shop pages.** It needs product data for whatever's in the cart, and the cart lives in `localStorage`, so the server can't know what to send — same problem `/cart` has, solved the same way with the shared index. The alternative was a fetch on open, which trades page weight for a loading state in the drawer. I chose instant, and it's noted in the code.
- **`SectionHeader` wraps awkwardly at 390px** — the action link sits beside a two-line title. Pre-existing, cosmetic, and touching it means changing a shared primitive across every section.

One housekeeping note: `recon/homepage updated.png` was an untracked file in your working tree — presumably the newer Amazon homepage you reviewed. `git add -A` swept it into this commit. Say the word if you'd rather it not be in the repo.

All four fixed, committed as `261957c`.

**1. Search category dropdown width** — A native `<select>` takes its width from its *widest* option, so a list containing "Clothing & Accessories" held the control at **171px even while it read "All"**, against roughly 64px in your Amazon screenshot. That's nearly a quarter of the search bar spent on one word. A hidden span carrying the selected label now sets the width with the select laid over it, so it sizes to the current selection: **54px on "All", 131px on "Home & Kitchen"**, capped at 150px so a long department name can't eat the field.

**2. Add to cart button** — Your reference shows the control becoming a quantity stepper once the item is in the cart, so Kartly does that now: a trash affordance at quantity 1 (as in your first image), a minus above it (as in your second), with "N in cart" between. It replaces a green "Added" flash that expired after 1400ms and reverted — which meant an added card ended up looking identical to one never touched. I kept the CTA's yellow border on the stepper so an added card still reads as acted on across a grid, and capped it at available stock. Built by giving the existing `QuantityStepper` an optional label rather than adding a second stepper.

**3. Currency display** — This was measurable. The symbol and decimals are raised by being small text top-aligned in a tall line box, but they *also* carried an extra `-0.6em` offset on top of that. Measured, the `$` sat **7.8px above the top of the digits** — floating clear of the number instead of against it, which is what you were seeing. In your Amazon capture the symbol overlaps the digits near their cap height. Removed the offset and let the alignment do the work; it now renders `$129⁰⁰` tight, like the reference.

**4. Text inconsistency (last image)** — `Badge` applied `whitespace-nowrap` to every variant. Correct for a boxed chip like `-35%`, wrong for a sentence: "Only 2 left in stock - order soon." is wider than a 160px carousel card, so it ran straight out of its card and collided with the neighbouring one — exactly the collision in your screenshot. Now only the boxed variants refuse to wrap; the text-only ones wrap inside their card. Verified zero stock lines overflow.

**Verification** — Build clean. Smoke 49/49, purchase 48/48, discovery 42/42, home 42/42, p1 54/54, fbt 23/23, drawer 69/69, responsive 189/189 across all seven widths. At 390px the stepper is 112px inside a 148px card with 36px of slack and no page overflow. Not deployed.

Two notes on judgement calls I made rather than asking:

- I gave the in-cart stepper the **yellow border** rather than the default grey. Your reference shows a yellow-outlined pill, and a grey one made an added card look quieter than an untouched one. Easy to revert if you'd rather it stay neutral.
- The stepper **sizes to its container**, matching whatever width the plain button had on that surface — full width in search rows, content width in carousel cards. That keeps the two states the same size rather than the card jumping when you add.

FINAL POLISH — STEP 8: FINAL EVALUATOR / RED-TEAM PASS

This is the final product refinement pass before submission.

P0 and P1 are complete.
The final visual/interaction polish has also been completed.

The goal of this step is NOT to add new features.

The goal is to experience Kartly as a first-time evaluator and fix only the remaining issues that would be visibly noticeable, confusing, broken, or unprofessional.

==================================================
CURRENT PRODUCT STANDARD
==================================================

Kartly should now feel like:

A polished, modern, Amazon-inspired e-commerce marketplace with its own branding.

The product should combine:

- familiar marketplace navigation
- strong product discovery
- dense but organized homepage content
- modern category merchandising
- consistent product cards
- coherent pricing/rating/deal presentation
- subtle interaction feedback
- polished cart drawer behavior
- responsive layouts
- simulated checkout
- clear order history
- useful empty/error states
- Kartly's own visual identity

Do NOT try to make it an exact Amazon clone.

Do NOT regress toward an older Amazon screenshot simply for pixel similarity.

==================================================
1. ACT AS A FIRST-TIME EVALUATOR
==================================================

Do not inspect the implementation first and assume everything works.

Instead, use the application like a real evaluator.

Start from the homepage.

Pretend you have never seen the project before.

Ask:

- Is it immediately obvious what this site does?
- Does the homepage feel like a real marketplace?
- Is the navigation understandable?
- Can I find a product quickly?
- Does search behave naturally?
- Does adding a product feel responsive?
- Does the cart drawer make sense?
- Can I reach checkout without confusion?
- Does checkout feel complete enough for a simulated marketplace?
- Can I understand my order afterward?
- Do mobile interactions feel intentional?
- Are there any moments where the UI feels unfinished?

Only fix genuine issues discovered through this process.

==================================================
2. TEST THE CORE SHOPPING JOURNEY
==================================================

Perform one complete representative journey:

Homepage
→ search
→ autocomplete
→ search results
→ filtering
→ sorting
→ product page
→ reviews
→ add to cart
→ cart drawer
→ cart
→ checkout
→ confirmation
→ orders

At each transition look for:

- broken navigation
- confusing labels
- dead buttons
- inconsistent states
- unexpected visual jumps
- incorrect totals
- stale cart information
- inconsistent currency formatting
- missing feedback
- awkward spacing
- obvious responsive problems

Do not redesign working screens.

==================================================
3. HEADER + NAVIGATION RED-TEAM
==================================================

Pay special attention to the header because this is the most frequently encountered UI.

Check:

- Kartly wordmark
- delivery/location area
- search field
- search category selector
- search button
- language/currency display
- account area
- returns/orders
- cart icon
- cart badge
- secondary navigation
- All/departments interaction

Verify that these elements feel like one coherent system.

Test:

- normal desktop
- mobile
- adding/removing products
- cart count updates
- cart drawer opening/closing
- navigation after cart interaction

If something feels inconsistent, fix it.

Do not introduce a major header redesign.

==================================================
4. HOMEPAGE RED-TEAM
==================================================

Inspect the homepage at approximately:

- 1440px
- 390px

Focus on the first impression.

Check:

- hero/category discovery
- department tiles
- product carousels
- category modules
- recommendation sections
- section spacing
- image consistency
- card proportions
- typography
- CTA clarity
- carousel controls
- horizontal scrolling
- footer

The homepage should feel:

- dense
- useful
- visually organized
- commerce-focused
- modern

It should NOT feel:

- like a SaaS landing page
- excessively empty
- overly rounded
- overloaded with animations
- visually chaotic

Do not add new homepage sections unless a clear existing problem requires it.

==================================================
5. PRODUCT DISCOVERY
==================================================

Test:

- normal search
- autocomplete
- no-result search
- filters
- multiple filters
- removable filter chips
- clear filters
- sorting
- pagination
- product cards
- product links

Verify that the URL state and visible UI remain synchronized.

Pay particular attention to whether the interface communicates what has been selected.

Do not redesign the filtering architecture.

==================================================
6. PRODUCT PAGE + REVIEWS
==================================================

Open a representative PDP.

Check:

- image gallery
- title
- brand
- rating
- review count
- price
- discount
- availability
- delivery information
- quantity selector
- Add to Cart
- Buy Now
- product information
- specifications
- reviews
- review filtering/sorting
- related products

Verify that the page does not contain obvious visual inconsistencies compared with search cards.

Do not add new PDP functionality.

==================================================
7. CART / CHECKOUT / ORDERS
==================================================

Test:

Add product
→ drawer
→ cart
→ quantity change
→ remove
→ checkout
→ simulated payment
→ confirmation
→ orders

Verify:

- quantities

- order display

Check that currency/price formatting is consistent throughout.

Do not introduce real payment processing.

==================================================
8. ALL / DEPARTMENT NAVIGATION
==================================================

Test the department navigation introduced during final polish.

Verify:

- desktop opening
- mobile opening
- keyboard accessibility
- Escape behavior
- outside-click behavior
- department links
- no dead links
- no duplicate/invisible navigation elements
- drawer does not cover important content incorrectly

The navigation should feel useful rather than decorative.

Fix only actual issues.

==================================================
9. SIGN-IN / ACCOUNT EXPERIENCE
==================================================

If the simulated sign-in experience is present, test:

- opening sign-in
- validation
- simulated successful sign-in
- header account state
- sign-out
- returning to shopping

Make sure shopping does NOT incorrectly require authentication.

This is a simulated experience only.

Do not add backend authentication.

==================================================
10. HELP / ERROR / EMPTY STATES
==================================================

Quickly verify:

- 404
- empty search
- no search results
- empty cart
- empty saved-for-later
- checkout with empty cart
- unknown product
- unknown order
- help page

Each should feel intentionally designed rather than like a raw framework error.

Do not add new state systems.

==================================================
11. MOBILE RED-TEAM
==================================================

Perform a quick mobile pass around:

390px.

Look specifically for:

- horizontal overflow
- clipped text
- inaccessible buttons
- oversized components
- broken drawers
- unusable search
- cramped product cards
- broken carousels
- footer problems
- checkout usability

Do not perform an exhaustive responsive sweep.

The existing P1 responsive QA has already been completed.

Only fix regressions or obvious remaining problems.

==================================================
12. VISUAL CONSISTENCY CHECK
==================================================

Look across the application for obvious inconsistencies in:

- typography
- spacing
- buttons
- border radius
- shadows
- badges
- prices
- ratings
- icons
- cards
- form controls
- focus states
- hover states
- transitions

If the same UI concept looks different in different parts of the application, prefer the existing shared primitive.

Do not create unnecessary new components.

==================================================
13. CONTENT / IMAGERY CHECK
==================================================

Perform a quick visual sanity check.

Look for:

- obviously incorrect category imagery
- broken images
- empty image boxes
- stretched images
- severe aspect-ratio problems
- obviously duplicated imagery where it looks accidental
- placeholder-looking content

Do NOT attempt another full image replacement project.

Only fix something if it is clearly damaging the evaluator's impression.

==================================================
14. WHAT NOT TO DO
==================================================

This is extremely important.

DO NOT:

- add major new features
- redesign the application
- rewrite the architecture
- replace the catalog
- replace the cart system
- introduce a backend
- introduce real authentication
- introduce real payments
- add external APIs
- change the brand
- copy Amazon branding
- add unnecessary animations
- add unnecessary dependencies
- refactor unrelated code
- chase pixel-perfect similarity with Amazon
- spend the task on exhaustive QA

If the application already does something correctly, leave it alone.

==================================================
15. MINIMAL QA ONLY
==================================================

After making fixes, perform only a lightweight smoke test.

Verify:

1. Production build succeeds.
2. Homepage loads.
3. Search works.
4. One product can be opened.
5. Add to Cart works.
6. Cart drawer works.
7. Cart works.
8. Checkout can be completed.
9. Order confirmation appears.
10. Orders page shows the order.
11. Mobile homepage loads around 390px.
12. No obvious console error caused by the final changes.

Do NOT rerun the full historical QA suite.

Do NOT spend significant time on automated regression testing unless a real problem is discovered.

==================================================
16. FINAL DECISION RULE
==================================================

For every issue you find, classify it as:

A. Clearly broken
→ FIX IT.

B. Clearly confusing or visibly unfinished
→ FIX IT.

C. Minor subjective preference
→ LEAVE IT ALONE.

D. Large architectural/design change
→ LEAVE IT ALONE.

The goal is refinement, not endless iteration.

==================================================
17. FINAL CLEANUP
==================================================

Before finishing:

- remove accidental debug output
- remove temporary test UI if any exists
- remove unused imports created during this pass
- remove obvious dead code created during this pass
- ensure .agent-logs/ remains intact
- do not modify capture configuration unnecessarily

Do not clean unrelated historical code.

==================================================
18. COMMIT
==================================================

If actual fixes were required, create one final focused commit:

fix: final evaluator polish

If no meaningful fixes are required, do not create a meaningless commit.

Do NOT deploy to Vercel.

Do NOT begin another feature phase.

==================================================
FINAL REPORT
==================================================

At the end, report:

2. Issues discovered
3. Issues fixed
4. Issues intentionally left unchanged
5. Minimal smoke-test result
6. Production build result
7. Final commit hash, if a commit was created

Do not continue beyond this step automatically.


[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=86 session=c4110ea9]
timestamp: 2026-09-21T08:45:37.877Z
model: claude-opus-5

## 1. Evaluator journey result

The full journey completes without a dead end: homepage → search → autocomplete (7 suggestions on a partial term) → results → facets → sort → PDP → reviews → add to cart → drawer → cart → checkout → confirmation → orders. URL state stayed synchronised with the UI throughout (`?q=`, `&sort=price-asc`, facet params). Cart cleared after the order, the order appeared in history, mobile had no overflow at 390px, and there were zero console or page errors across the whole walk.

It reads as a real marketplace. What it does is obvious within a second of landing.

## 2. Issues discovered

1. **`office-09` carried a `readysetimages.com` watermark stamped across the frame.** Worst of the lot, because department tiles take the most-reviewed product of their category — so that watermark was also on the homepage, above the fold.
2. **`books-10` carried a `magneticman.com` watermark**, on a photo of a man in a Tokyo manga shop standing in for a crime thriller paperback.
3. **An enabled `<select>` wore the app's disabled colour.** The shared control base sets `disabled:bg-[#f0f2f2]`, and `Select` used that exact grey as its *normal* background — most visible in the checkout address row, where State sat between two white inputs and read as switched off.
4. **`npm run lint` failed**: `next lint` was removed in Next 16, so it parsed "lint" as a directory and errored with `Invalid project directory: D:\8x Amazon\lint`.

## 3. Issues fixed

All four. The two images were replaced with verified CC0 photographs through the existing pin mechanism, under new filenames so no cache can serve the old ones. Worth knowing for the future: Openverse's CC0 pool has a lot of this — several stock sites publish watermarked previews under a free licence, and a licence filter cannot see a watermark. My first replacement candidate had the same problem from a sibling domain, which is why I checked each by eye.

There's no ESLint config or binary in the project, and adding one means a new dependency this pass forbids, so I removed the dead script rather than leave a command that always errors. `typecheck` remains.

## 4. Left unchanged, deliberately

- **The PDP gallery.** I initially read its four thumbnails as duplicates and was wrong — at 3× they're genuinely distinct crops of the one photograph, and selecting one really does reframe the main image. It only looks repetitive on soft, low-contrast source photos.
- **Order IDs.** My first test order came out `KTL-AZSZK0-KTL` and looked like a doubled prefix. Generating a batch showed it's a 1-in-46656 coincidence in the random suffix, not a format bug.
- **Several category tiles are semantically loose** — Computers is a USB hub photographed unappealingly, Beauty is a gift arrangement, Clothing is a wristwatch. All clean, licensed, no watermarks. Re-sourcing them is the image project this pass was explicitly told not to start.
- Minor spacing and wording preferences throughout — category C.

## 5. Smoke test

**12/12**, exactly the checks the brief lists: build, homepage, search, product page, add to cart, drawer, badge, cart, checkout completion, confirmation with order number, cart cleared, orders listing, mobile homepage at 390px without overflow, no console errors.

## 6. Production build

Clean, from a cleared `.next`. TypeScript passes.

## 7. Commit

`4e0120d` — `fix: final evaluator polish`

## 8. Ready for deployment?

**Yes, with two caveats you should decide on first — one of which I'd treat as blocking.**

**Blocking, in my view:** the git history still contains the semi-nude celebrity paparazzi image removed earlier. Commit `fb84e95` took it out of the working tree, but the blob is reachable from earlier commits, so it ships the moment the repo goes public. That needs a history rewrite (`git filter-repo`) or a fresh initial commit. I haven't done it because it's destructive and touches your `.agent-logs/` commits — but I would not make this repo public until it's handled.

**Worth knowing, not blocking:** imagery is the weakest part of the product. The catalogue is contextual stock photography rather than product cutouts on white, which is what makes some category tiles read oddly. I fixed the two that were actively unprofessional; the rest are a quality ceiling, not a defect.

Everything else — build, journey, states, responsive, accessibility of the drawers, simulated auth and payment — I'd put in front of an evaluator as-is. Stopping here as instructed.

Fixed, `9816c5d`.

**Why it wasn't opening:** I'd scoped the panel to buy-box adds on purpose — but that was the wrong call, and your screenshot shows why. Ours was *modal* (scrim, focus trap, scroll lock), so opening it on a quick add meant anyone adding three items from one results page had to dismiss it twice in between. I worked around that by not opening it, instead of fixing the cause.

Amazon's panel isn't modal — in your screenshot the results are fully visible and clickable right next to it. So ours isn't either now: no scrim, no scroll lock, no focus trap, no focus stealing. Escape, the close button, an outside click and navigation all dismiss it. Every add-to-cart opens it — listing cards, buy box, frequently-bought-together.

Verified against your reference: panel on the right, page live behind it, item with stepper, subtotal, Go to Cart, and the card itself showing "1 in cart".

**One trade worth knowing:** it's no longer a focus-trapped dialog. That's the correct semantics for a panel that deliberately leaves the page usable, and the card's own "N in cart" stepper still confirms the add where the shopper is already looking — but it is a reduction in keyboard-modal rigour versus what was there before.

**One behaviour difference at 390px:** the panel covers the viewport, so a second add there means closing it first. Same as the reference on mobile.

Drawer smoke 48/48, final smoke 12/12, purchase 48/48, discovery 42/42. Build clean, not deployed.

[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=88 session=c4110ea9]
timestamp: 2026-09-21T09:48:51.555Z

**Answer to your question: the persistence was quick. Done — `0250d36`.**

The part that would have taken real time is making the page *reflow* around the panel, and it turns out that isn't needed: in your first screenshot Amazon's panel **overlays** the page too — the "Level up your PC here" carousel is clipped behind it. So the overlay we already had was right; only the persistence was missing.

**What changed:** it was behaving like a notification — appeared on add, vanished on the next outside click or navigation. Now it's a standing sidebar. An outside click no longer closes it, navigation no longer closes it. Escape, the close button and the collapse handle do. When collapsed with items still in the cart, a `◀` tab sits on the right edge to bring it back without adding anything — the same affordance in your screenshot.

**Two places suppress it,** and the first one is a real find rather than a nicety: on `/cart` the panel would sit exactly over the summary rail carrying "Proceed to checkout". On `/checkout` it would hand back the ways out of the page that checkout strips on purpose.

**Two limits worth knowing:**
- The open state is in memory, so a **hard reload starts collapsed**. The reopen tab is there whenever the cart has contents, so it's one click back. Persisting it across reloads would mean putting UI state in `localStorage` — doable, but a different decision about what deserves persistence.
- **At 390px the panel covers the viewport**, so navigating or adding again means collapsing first. The two smoke checks that depend on reaching the page behind it are scoped to desktop for that reason.

**Not done:** the "*X* was removed from Shopping Cart" message in your second screenshot. That needs tracking the removed item's title as transient state — maybe ten minutes, and I left it since you asked for the quick version. Currently removing the last item shows the empty-cart state instead. Say the word if you want it.

Drawer smoke 49/49, final smoke 12/12, build clean, not deployed.

timestamp: 2026-09-25T10:53:55.259Z
model: claude-opus-5

Audit complete. No application code, packages or files were touched.

## Checkpoint status

| | |
|---|---|
| Branch before | `master` |
| Working tree | **Not clean** — one file: `.agent-logs/2026-09-20_15-58-14_c4110ea9.md` |
| Nature of that change | The live capture hook appending this session (+63/−2, the −2 being frontmatter counters). Additive, must not be discarded. |
| Remote sync | `master` exactly level with `origin/master` (0 ahead, 0 behind) |
| Checkpoint commit | `5d932a3` — pushed to `github.com/FarhanFK2003/kartly-amazon-rebuild` |
| Branch now | **`full-stack-revamp`** created from `5d932a3`, carrying the log delta across |

The tree was already dirty when you asked, so I couldn't report "clean". I deliberately did **not** commit or stash the log — that would have been a change you didn't ask for. It travels with the new branch untouched.

## Versions

Next **16.3.5** (App Router, Turbopack) · React **19.3.0** · TypeScript **5.9.3** · Tailwind **4.3.3** (CSS-first `@theme`, no `tailwind.config`) · Zustand **5.0.15** · Fuse.js **7.5.0** · Playwright 1.63 (dev only)

## Architecture

**Structure** — 17 route files in 3 route groups: `(shop)` full chrome, `(checkout)` stripped chrome, `(auth)` minimal. 46 components, 17 lib modules, 18 scripts.

**Data** — One file: `data/catalog.json`, 452KB, 10 categories / 120 products / **672 reviews embedded inside the product records**. Generated deterministically by `scripts/generate-catalog.mjs` (mulberry32 PRNG + FNV-1a). Imported directly as a module by `lib/catalog.ts`; every read is a synchronous in-memory array scan. No pagination at the data layer, no IDs beyond string slugs, no referential structure — reviews and variants are nested arrays, not relations.

**Reviews** — No standalone entity. `product.reviews[]` with `{id, author, rating, title, body, date, verified, helpful}`, plus a precomputed `ratingHistogram` and `reviewCount` on the parent. Filtering/sorting is entirely client-side over the nested array.

**Cart** — `lib/store/cart.ts`, Zustand + `persist` → `localStorage["kartly.cart"]`. Stores only `{productId, variantId, qty, saved}`. Product data is joined at render time via `getCartIndex()` (whole catalogue, ~13KB gzipped, shipped into every shop page for the mini-cart).

**Checkout / orders** — `lib/commerce.ts` is the single source of commercial truth: `COMMERCE` constants (threshold 3500, shipping 599, tax 8.25%), `resolveLines`, `computeTotals`, `toOrderItems` (price snapshots), `generateOrderId` (`KTL-{base36 stamp}-{random}`). Orders persist to `localStorage["kartly.orders"]`. Checkout draft in `kartly.checkout`. Confirmation is a client route reading local state.

**Search / filter / sort** — `lib/search.ts`, ~370 lines, entirely URL-driven: `parseFacets` / `facetsToHref` / `toggleFacet` / `searchCatalog`. Multi-select facets, 5 sort keys, `PAGE_SIZE = 16`, facet counts computed by applying every facet *except* the one being counted. Fuse.js fuzzy matching. All in-process, per request, over the full array.

**Auth** — Simulated only. `localStorage["kartly.auth"]` holds a display name + typed identifier. No session, no token, **password validated for shape then discarded**. Nothing in the store requires it.

**API routes** — Exactly one: `app/api/suggest/route.ts` (GET, reads the same static catalogue, cached 300s/3600s). That is the entire server surface.

**Env / deployment** — **Zero** environment variables referenced anywhere. No `.env` files. No `vercel.json`, Docker, or CI config. `next.config.ts` sets only AVIF/WebP image formats.

**Commands** — `dev`, `build`, `start`, `typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`), `catalog`, `images`. **No test runner** — the 15 `qa-*.mjs` scripts are standalone Playwright programs run manually against a live server on `127.0.0.1:3000`.

**Capture** — `.claude/settings.json` wires `UserPromptSubmit` + `Stop` to `.claude/hooks/capture.js` (296 lines) in exec form, because the project path contains a space. 4 log files, largest 219KB. `.gitignore` carries an explicit note that `.agent-logs/` is intentionally not ignored.

## Must be protected during the redesign

1. **`.agent-logs/` and `.claude/`** — assignment deliverable. The hook uses `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` exec-form args specifically to survive the space in the path; any restructure that moves the project root breaks capture silently.
2. **Image licensing chain** — `public/products/` (120 files), `data/image-sources.json`, `ATTRIBUTION.md`. These are generated together by `fetch-images.mjs`, which also holds hand-verified `PINNED` ids and `IMAGE_FILE_OVERRIDES`. If catalogue generation is rewritten without carrying these forward, the repo loses its provenance record for publicly redistributed assets — a legal problem, not a cosmetic one.
3. **`lib/commerce.ts`** — totals, tax, shipping and order snapshotting. Whatever the backend becomes, this is the definition that the cart, drawer, checkout and orders all agree on. Porting it to the server is fine; forking it is how a drawer and a cart start disagreeing about a subtotal.
4. **URL-as-state in `lib/search.ts`** — facets, sort and pagination are fully expressible as a shareable URL. Worth preserving through a move to SQL.
5. **The QA scripts** — ~400 accumulated assertions encoding real defects found and fixed. They target selectors and ARIA labels, so a visual redesign will break many; they should be migrated, not deleted.

## Two conflicts you should decide on before I touch code

**The interface requirement is a direct conflict.** The revised assignment asks for *"an interface designed by the candidate with their own layout and visual design."* Kartly's current layout is a deliberate, close replica of Amazon's, built from `/recon` screenshots and refined over several passes explicitly toward that reference. Preserving the frontend as-is and adding a backend would satisfy requirements 2–5 and **fail requirement 1**. This is the largest piece of work in the revamp, and it's the opposite of what the last eight steps optimised for.

**"No mock data" needs a definition.** Seeding a real database from `catalog.json` is legitimate — that's fixture data. Serving `catalog.json` from a route handler would not be, and neither would the simulated auth or the simulated payment, both of which currently fake a server response on the client. Those three are the specific things that must become real.

Also still outstanding from before: the removed inappropriate image remains reachable in git history, which matters if this repo goes public.

I've made no implementation changes and installed nothing. Ready for your direction on the interface question before planning further.

# Kartly Frontend Redesign Specification

No files were modified. This is the spec only.

---

## 1. Kartly design concept

**"Shop the shelf. Decide with facts."**

Kartly's current build competes with Amazon on Amazon's terms — maximum density, chrome-heavy navigation, decoration carrying the brand. It loses that comparison by definition, because it's a replica.

The redesign competes on a different axis: **a calm marketplace that helps you decide.** Same catalogue, same commerce, opposite posture.

Three principles drive every decision below:

1. **Chrome recedes, product advances.** The current header is two rows, 99px, and carries eleven controls including a full department nav. The new header is one row, 56px, and carries four. Departments move out of the header entirely. Everything reclaimed goes to product.
2. **Decisions are made with facts, not pressure.** The current PDP leads with a yellow buy box and urgency copy. The new PDP leads with a comparison-grade spec summary and a review facts bar. Deals are stated, not shouted.
3. **One thing is prominent per screen.** Currently the homepage has a hero carousel, two four-up promo rows, five product rails and a department grid, all at the same visual weight with uniform 16px gaps. The new composition has a deliberate weight order and a rhythm that varies.

**What makes it recognisably Kartly and not Amazon:** a warm paper ground instead of grey, deep teal instead of navy-and-yellow, a serif display face against a neutral UI face, rounded-rectangle controls instead of pills, plain tabular prices instead of the raised-symbol signature, departments in a left rail instead of a top mega-nav, filters across the top instead of down the left, a grid-first results page instead of list rows, and a bottom tab bar on mobile instead of a hamburger drawer.

---

## 2. Visual design system

### Colour

Replaces navy `#131921` / `#232f3e` and yellow `#ffd814` entirely.

```
--paper:        #FBFAF8   page ground (warm, not grey)
--surface:      #FFFFFF   cards, panels
--surface-sunk: #F3F1ED   wells, inactive tabs, skeletons
--line:         #E6E2DB   hairline borders (1px, does the work shadows used to)
--line-strong:  #CFC9BF   form control borders

--ink:          #1A1A18   primary text
--ink-2:        #5C5951   secondary text
--ink-3:        #8B877E   tertiary, metadata

--brand:        #14594F   deep teal — primary CTA, active states, links
--brand-hover:  #0E453D
--brand-tint:   #E8F0EE   selected chip fill, active nav rail item

--accent:       #B4443A   terracotta — discounts, low stock, destructive
--accent-tint:  #FBEEEC
--success:      #1E7A4C   in stock, free shipping, order placed
--focus:        #14594F   3px ring at 40% opacity
```

Contrast: `--ink` on `--paper` is ~15:1; `--ink-2` ~6.8:1; white on `--brand` ~8.1:1. All exceed WCAG AA at body size.

### Typography

Two families via `next/font` — **no new package**, `next/font` ships with Next.

- **Display — Fraunces** (variable serif, optical sizing). Section headings, PDP title, page titles. This is the single strongest identity signal and the clearest break from Amazon's sans-only stack.
- **UI/body — Inter** (variable). Everything else.
- **Numerals** — Inter with `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums lining` on every price, rating and quantity so columns align.

> Prefer `next/font/local` with the woff2 files committed to `public/fonts/`, not `next/font/google`. The project already refuses runtime dependencies on third-party hosts for imagery; fonts should follow the same rule, and it removes a network requirement from `next build`.

```
display-lg  34/40  Fraunces 500   page titles
display-md  26/32  Fraunces 500   section headings
display-sm  20/26  Fraunces 500   card group headings, PDP subsections
body-lg     16/24  Inter 400
body        14/21  Inter 400      default
body-sm     13/18  Inter 400      metadata
label       12/16  Inter 500 +0.01em uppercase — eyebrows, facet groups
```

Current scale runs 11/12/13/14/15/16/17/18/21/24/26/28/31px across eight heading roles. New scale is **seven sizes total**.

### Shape, space, elevation

- **Radius:** `sm 6px` (chips, inputs, badges) · `md 10px` (cards, images) · `lg 16px` (panels, sheets). **Buttons are 8px rounded rectangles, not pills.** The pill button is an Amazon signature; dropping it changes the read of every screen.
- **Space:** 4px base. Component padding `12/16/20`. **Section rhythm `48px` desktop / `32px` mobile**, replacing today's uniform `16px` everywhere — this alone restores hierarchy the current page lacks.
- **Elevation:** borders only. Shadows exist on exactly three things: the search overlay, the filter sheet, the mini-cart panel. No card shadows.

### Price presentation — a deliberate break

Retire the raised currency symbol and raised decimals. That typographic signature is the most recognisable Amazon marketplace mark in the current build.

```
Regular:   $129.00                      Inter 600, tabular
Discount:  $129.00  $234.99  −45%       current • struck ink-3 • terracotta chip
```

`PriceBlock` keeps its API (`cents`, `listPrice`, `dealPercent`, `size`) and its role as the single price definition; only its internal composition changes. `splitPrice()` stays exported for any caller that still wants parts, but nothing in the new UI uses it.

---

## 3. New information architecture

### Routes

Preserved exactly — **every URL contract in `lib/search.ts` survives**: `q`, `i`, `brand`, `price`, `rating`, `deals`, `sort`, `page`.

| Route | Change |
|---|---|
| `/` | Recomposed (§5) |
| `/s` | Recomposed, same params (§6) |
| `/dp/[id]` | Recomposed (§8) |
| `/cart` `/checkout` `/order-confirmation/[orderId]` `/orders` `/signin` `/help` | Recomposed (§9) |
| **`/browse`** | **New.** Department index page. Absorbs the department mega-drawer. |

`/browse` exists because departments leave the header. It is a real page — indexable, linkable, shareable — where the drawer was an ephemeral overlay reachable only by click.

### Navigation model

| | Current | New |
|---|---|---|
| Departments | Header sub-nav row + modal drawer | `/browse` page + **left nav rail on `/s` and `/browse`** (desktop) |
| Search | Inline header input + dropdown | **Full-screen overlay panel**, opened from a header trigger or `⌘K` / `/` |
| Mobile primary nav | Hamburger → left drawer | **Bottom tab bar:** Home · Browse · Search · Cart · Orders |
| Filters | Left rail (desktop) / left drawer (mobile) | **Top facet bar of chips** + right slide-over sheet, both widths |
| Mini-cart | Full-height right overlay | **Docked rail that reflows the grid ≥1280px**, overlay below |

The structural theme: **vertical chrome moves out of the top and into context.** The top of the viewport is the most valuable space on a commerce page, and the current build spends 99px of it on navigation before any product appears.

---

## 4. Header / navigation redesign

### Desktop — one row, 56px (from two rows, 99px)

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Kartly    Browse ▾      [ ⌕ Search products        ⌘K ]    Help  ⌂  ⊙ │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   wordmark  nav trigger    search trigger (button, not input)    orders account cart
```

- **Ground:** `--surface` white with a `--line` bottom hairline. Not a dark bar. The dark navy band is an Amazon signature and a large block of non-product colour.
- **Browse** opens a lightweight two-column popover (departments + a few curated entries) and links to `/browse`. It replaces the 380px full-height modal drawer.
- **Search is a trigger button**, not a live input (§C below).
- **Right cluster collapses from six controls to four:** Help, Orders, Account, Cart. "Deliver to United States" and the language/currency menu move into the footer and the account menu respectively — neither is actionable in a single-locale demo, and both currently occupy prime header space.
- **Cart** shows a count badge as a small brand-filled counter at the icon's top-right, replacing the overlapping orange numeral.
- **The entire department sub-nav row is deleted.** −39px of permanent chrome.

### Sticky behaviour

Header is sticky. On scroll past 200px it compresses to 48px and the wordmark reduces — a single CSS transition, no JS scroll listener beyond an `IntersectionObserver` sentinel.

### Mobile — 52px app bar + bottom tabs

App bar: wordmark, search icon, cart icon. Everything else moves to the bottom tab bar, which is `fixed bottom-0`, 56px + `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, five items, active item in `--brand`.

---

## 5. Homepage redesign

The current page is: hero carousel → department grid → deals rail → 4-up promo → rail → 4-up promo → rail → recently viewed → rail → rail. Ten modules, uniform weight, uniform 16px gaps.

The new page is **six modules with deliberate weight order**:

**1 · Value strip** (32px) — a thin bar directly under the header: free delivery threshold, 30-day returns, simulated-store notice. Honest, useful, and it retires the footer-bound trust copy.

**2 · Promo pair** — replaces the 4-slide autoplay hero carousel. **Two static side-by-side panels** (2:1 and 1:1), one editorial and one deal-led. No autoplay, no rotation, no indicator dots, no arrow controls.
> *Why:* the current carousel autoplays every 6s, needs a reduced-motion guard, four slide images, arrow buttons and dot controls, and shows three-quarters of its content to nobody. Two static panels deliver the same promotional surface with none of that machinery.

**3 · Category mosaic** — replaces the uniform 5-column department grid. An **asymmetric 12-column mosaic**: two feature tiles spanning 5 and 4 columns with imagery, eight compact text-led tiles. Creates the hierarchy a 10-item uniform grid cannot.

**4 · Shelves** — three product shelves (`Deals`, `Best sellers`, `Popular in Electronics`), each a horizontal scroller with a heading, one-line rationale and a "See all" link. Same data, new card (§7), 48px rhythm between.

**5 · Guide band** — one editorial module between shelves, built from existing `lib/help.ts` content ("How delivery works", "What's simulated here"). Breaks shelf monotony with something genuinely useful and needs no new data.

**6 · Recently viewed** — retained, moved to just above the footer where it belongs.

Net: ten modules → six. Rails five → three.

---

## 6. Product discovery redesign

### `/browse` — new

Full-page department index. Each of the 10 departments gets a row: name, blurb, product count, four representative products, "See all". This is the drawer's content, made addressable.

### `/s` — listing and search results

**One template for both**, as today (browse mode vs query mode), but recomposed:

```
┌──────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Nav rail │  Electronics                          12 products    │  ← page header
│          ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ All      │  [Filters 2] [Brand ▾] [Price ▾] [★4+ ▾] [Deals]  ⇅  │  ← facet bar (sticky)
│ Electr.▸ ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Comput.  │  Brand: Kestrel ✕   Under $50 ✕        Clear all     │  ← active chips
│ Home…    ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ …        │  ┌────┐ ┌────┐ ┌────┐ ┌────┐                        │
│          │  │card│ │card│ │card│ │card│    ← GRID, not rows     │
│ 200px    │  └────┘ └────┘ └────┘ └────┘                        │
└──────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Three structural changes:

- **G/H — Results become a grid.** Currently desktop search results are full-width list rows. New default is a **4-up grid** (3 at 1024, 2 at 640, 2 at 390), with a **density toggle** offering the list view as an option, persisted per-viewer in `localStorage`. Grid is the honest default for a catalogue of photographed products with short titles.
- **I — Filters move from the left rail to the top.** A sticky **facet bar of dropdown chips** with a "Filters" button opening a **right slide-over sheet** carrying the full facet set. Identical on desktop and mobile — one component, one code path, no separate mobile drawer. Active facets render as **removable chips** on their own line, with "Clear all". The left rail is now *departments*, which are navigation, not filtering — the current build conflates the two.
- **J — Sort** moves into the facet bar as a right-aligned control with an explicit current value (`Sort: Price, low to high`), replacing a bare `<select>` labelled "Featured".
- **K — Pagination** becomes **"Load more" (appending, pushing `?page=`) plus a numbered pager** at the foot. URL state and deep-linkability are preserved exactly; `PAGE_SIZE = 16` is unchanged.

### C — Search redesign

The inline header input with a dropdown becomes a **full-screen overlay panel**:

- Opens from the header trigger, `⌘K`, or `/`.
- Layout: large input, then **three zones** — *Suggestions* (from `/api/suggest`), *Departments*, *Products* (image, title, price — richer than the current single-line rows).
- Empty state shows recent searches (localStorage) and popular departments — the current dropdown shows nothing until you type.
- **Progressive enhancement is mandatory and non-negotiable:** the overlay contains a real `<form action="/s" method="get">`. With JS off or pre-hydration, the header trigger is a link to `/s`, and `/s` renders a real inline search form. The current build's search-without-JS behaviour must not regress — it was a bug once already.
- Keeps the full WAI-ARIA combobox pattern: `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, roving highlight.

---

## 7. Product-card redesign

Current card: image, badge, stock warning, title, stars, price, delivery, CTA — eight stacked elements, all the same weight, ~6 lines of text.

New card — **three tiers with explicit weight**:

```
┌─────────────────────┐
│                     │
│      image 1:1      │   ← object-cover, 10px radius, --surface-sunk ground
│                 [♡] │   ← hover-only save affordance
│  −45%               │   ← discount chip, bottom-left, terracotta
├─────────────────────┤
│ Orvan Perch Modular │   ← TIER 1: title, 2 lines max, ink, 14/20
│ Cat Tree, 150cm     │
│ $129.00  $234.99    │   ← TIER 1: price, 16px semibold tabular
│                     │
│ ★ 4.2 · 2,823       │   ← TIER 2: rating, one line, ink-3, 13px
│ Free delivery Fri   │   ← TIER 2: single delivery line
├─────────────────────┤
│ [    Add to cart   ]│   ← TIER 3: full-width, appears on hover ≥1024
└─────────────────────┘
```

Changes that matter:

- **Title above price**, price immediately under it. Current order buries the price below stars and badges.
- **Stars collapse to a compact `★ 4.2 · 2,823`** — a single line instead of five drawn glyphs plus a count plus a chevron.
- **Discount moves onto the image** as a chip; the `-45%` badge leaves the price block.
- **Low-stock warning moves to the delivery line** and only renders at ≤3 (from ≤9). At ≤9 across 120 products it fires constantly and reads as manufactured urgency.
- **CTA is revealed on hover on desktop, always visible on touch.** Reduces the resting visual weight of a 16-card grid by a third.
- **The in-cart stepper state is preserved** — the card swaps the CTA for a stepper reading "2 in cart", which is genuinely good and was built deliberately.

One card component, three variants (`grid` · `shelf` · `row`), as today.

---

## 8. PDP redesign

Current: three columns (gallery · info · buy box), then FBT, then product info, then reviews, then related.

New: **two columns with a sticky decision panel**, and a restructured body.

```
┌──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
│  breadcrumb                                        │
│  Nightjar — Crime Thriller                         │  ← display-lg, full width
│  Ashgrove Books · ★3.8 (4,630) · #4 in Books       │  ← one meta line
├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
│                          │ ┌─────────────────────┐ │
│    gallery               │ │ $10.00  $13.99 −30% │ │  ← sticky decision card
│    (main + filmstrip     │ │ In stock            │ │
│     BELOW, not beside)   │ │ Free del. Sat 26    │ │
│                          │ │ Qty [1▾]            │ │
│                          │ │ [ Add to cart     ] │ │  ← brand solid
│                          │ │ [ Buy now         ] │ │  ← outline, NOT orange
│                          │ │ ─────────────────── │ │
│                          │ │ Ships from · Kartly │ │
│                          │ └─────────────────────┘ │
├──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┤
│  At a glance        ← NEW: 4-up spec facts strip   │
│  Format · Pages · Series · Publisher               │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  [Details] [Specifications] [Reviews] [Delivery]   │  ← anchored section nav
│  …                                                 │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- **Title and meta span full width** above the fold instead of being trapped in a middle column.
- **Gallery filmstrip moves below the main image**, not beside it — recovers horizontal space and works identically on mobile.
- **"At a glance"** is a new 4-up facts strip built from the existing `specs` object. Supports the "decide with facts" concept using data already in `catalog.json`.
- **Two CTAs, clearly ranked:** brand-solid `Add to cart`, outline `Buy now`. Today they are yellow and orange — two saturated fills competing at equal weight.
- **Anchored section nav** replaces one long scroll, with `Details / Specifications / Reviews / Delivery`.
- **Frequently bought together** stays, restyled, moved below the fold.

### M — Reviews redesign

Currently: a rating summary, a histogram, filter controls, then a list of review bodies.

New — **facts bar then cards**:

1. **Summary band (horizontal, not a sidebar):** large average, the 5→1 distribution as horizontal bars, and — new — **attribute bars** derived from existing review ratings (e.g. *Value 4.1 · Quality 4.4*), computed, not invented.
2. **Filter chips** — `All · 5★ · 4★ · Verified · With photos`, replacing the select controls. Star filter state stays URL-bound.
3. **Review cards** on `--surface` with a hairline, not undivided list rows: author, verified pill, date, stars, title, body, helpful control.

All 672 existing reviews render unchanged; only presentation moves.

---

## 9. Cart / checkout / order redesign

### N — Cart

Two-pane layout retained (it's correct), recomposed:

- Line items become **cards with a hairline**, not divided rows.
- **Quantity, save-for-later and remove group into one control cluster** on the right of each line, instead of a row of text links under the title.
- Summary rail: free-shipping meter at top, then a **clean totals table** (subtotal / shipping / estimated tax / total) — the cart currently shows only a subtotal, while checkout shows the full breakdown. Same numbers, both places, from `computeTotals()`.
- Saved-for-later becomes a collapsed section with a count.

### O — Mini-cart

You asked for the standing sidebar last week, and its **persistence is preserved** — it stays open while the cart has contents, across navigation, dismissed only deliberately, with the reopen tab.

What changes is composition: at **≥1280px it becomes a docked rail that reflows the page** (grid drops from 4-up to 3-up) instead of overlaying content. Below 1280px it stays an overlay exactly as now.

> This directly addresses the limitation flagged when it shipped — that it permanently covers the right 400px. It is the one place where reflow is worth the layout cost, and it's cheap here because the results grid is already column-count driven.

Suppression on `/cart` and `/checkout` is retained.

### P — Checkout

**Three-step accordion → single page with a sticky summary.**

```
┌────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
│ 1 Delivery address             │  Order summary   │
│   [ inline form, always open ] │  (sticky)        │
│ 2 Payment method               │  Items    $49.99 │
│   [ inline, always open ]      │  Shipping  FREE  │
│ 3 Review                       │  Tax       $4.12 │
│   [ line items ]               │  ─────────────── │
│                                │  Total    $54.11 │
│                                │  [ Place order ] │
└────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────┘
```

All three sections visible and editable at once, with completion ticks. Removes the "Use this address → collapse → expand next" ceremony. The simulated-payment notice becomes a persistent inline banner rather than footer small print.

`lib/validation.ts` and `lib/store/checkout.ts` are untouched — this is layout only.

### Q — Order confirmation

Already strong; keep the structure. Recompose to the new system, lead with a **delivery estimate as the hero fact** (it's what people actually want), order number secondary.

### R — Order history

Currently a flat card list. New: **grouped by month**, each order a card with a status chip (`Arriving Thu 24` / `Delivered`), thumbnail strip, total, and `View details` / `Buy again`. Empty state retained — it's good.

### S — Footer

Current footer is a 4-column Amazon-style link farm plus a "Back to top" bar. New: **three columns** (Shop / Help / About), a locale + currency line, and the simulated-store disclosure as a proper statement, not fine print. "Back to top" bar removed — the sticky header already returns you.

---

## 10. Mobile / responsive redesign

Breakpoints unchanged: `390 · 480 · 640 · 768 · 1024 · 1280 · 1440`.

| Area | Change |
|---|---|
| **T — Navigation** | **Bottom tab bar** (Home/Browse/Search/Cart/Orders) replaces the hamburger + left drawer. Thumb-reachable, always visible, no modal. |
| App bar | 52px: wordmark, search, cart only |
| Search | Overlay is full-screen on mobile — the same component, not a separate mobile path |
| Filters | The **same right slide-over sheet** as desktop. Removes the separate mobile filter drawer entirely. |
| Results | 2-up grid at 390 (from single-column rows) |
| PDP | Sticky **bottom action bar** with price + Add to cart once the decision card scrolls out |
| Checkout | Sections stack; summary becomes a sticky bottom bar with expandable totals |
| Safe areas | `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` on tab bar and all sticky bottom bars |

The big win: **three components delete their separate mobile implementations** (department drawer, filter drawer, mobile header block). One implementation each, responsive.

---

## 11. Component architecture changes

### New primitives — `components/ui/`

| Component | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `Chip` | Facet chips, filter pills, status chips, review filters |
| `Sheet` | Right slide-over. One implementation for filters, mini-cart, browse |
| `Overlay` | Full-screen panel — search |
| `Shelf` | Horizontal product scroller with heading + action |
| `StatBar` | Horizontal distribution bars — review histogram, attribute ratings |
| `Pager` | Load-more + numbered pagination |
| `Tabs` | Anchored section nav (PDP, reviews) |
| `Skeleton` | Shape-matched loading placeholders |

### New chrome — `components/chrome/`

`AppBar` · `BrowsePopover` · `NavRail` · `SearchOverlay` · `BottomTabs`

### Retired (deleted only after their replacement ships)

| Current | Replaced by |
|---|---|
| `SubNav` | — (departments leave the header) |
| `DepartmentDrawer` | `BrowsePopover` + `/browse` + `NavRail` |
| `DrawerTrigger`, `lib/store/navDrawer.ts` | — |
| `HeroCarousel` | `PromoPair` (static) |
| `CardRow` | `CategoryMosaic` |
| `ProductRail` | `Shelf` |
| `FilterShell` | `FacetBar` + `Sheet` |

### Preserved, restyled internally

`Button` · `PriceBlock` · `StarRating` · `Badge` · `Field` · `QuantityStepper` · `SectionHeader` · `ProductCard` · `CartDrawer` (behaviour intact, composition changed)

### New: the test-ID contract

`lib/testids.ts` — a single exported map of stable semantic identifiers consumed by components *and* QA scripts. This is the mechanism that makes §13 possible and prevents the next redesign from invalidating the suite again.

```ts
export const TID = {
  productCard: "product-card",
  productCardTitle: "product-card-title",
  productCardPrice: "product-card-price",
  addToCart: "add-to-cart",
  cartQtyStepper: "cart-qty-stepper",
  facetBar: "facet-bar",
  facetChip: "facet-chip",
  activeFilterChip: "active-filter-chip",
  miniCart: "mini-cart",
  miniCartSubtotal: "mini-cart-subtotal",
  searchOverlay: "search-overlay",
  // …
} as const;
```

---

## 12. Data and functionality that must remain unchanged

**Absolutely untouched in Phase A:**

1. `data/catalog.json` — shape, 120 products, 672 embedded reviews, all fields. Phase C migrates it; Phase A must not alter it.
2. `public/products/` — all 120 images, filenames included. Filenames are cache keys and are referenced by `catalog.json`.
3. `data/image-sources.json`, `ATTRIBUTION.md`, and the `PINNED` / `IMAGE_FILE_OVERRIDES` workflow in `scripts/fetch-images.mjs`. This is the licensing provenance chain for publicly redistributed assets — a legal record, not a convenience.
4. `lib/commerce.ts` — `COMMERCE` constants, `resolveLines`, `computeTotals`, `toOrderItems`, `generateOrderId`. **No formula is reimplemented in a component.** When it moves server-side in Phase D/F, it becomes a shared module imported by both server and client, never a copy.
5. `lib/search.ts` URL contract — `q`, `i`, `brand`, `price`, `rating`, `deals`, `sort`, `page`; `PAGE_SIZE = 16`; facet counting semantics (count each facet with all *other* facets applied). Presentation changes; the contract does not.
6. `lib/catalog.ts` public function surface — the seam Phase E will swap for API calls. Keep signatures stable so that swap is mechanical.
7. `/api/suggest` request/response contract.
8. `.claude/` hooks and `.agent-logs/`. Not modified, not gitignored, not relocated. The hook's exec-form args depend on the project root; **the root does not move**.
9. Store keys — `kartly.cart`, `kartly.orders`, `kartly.auth`, `kartly.checkout`, `kartly.recentlyViewed`. Phase F migrates them server-side; Phase A must not rename them or existing carts vanish.
10. Behaviours known to be load-bearing: the mount-gated hydration guard (`useIsMounted`), the `<Suspense>` boundary around anything reading `useSearchParams` (its absence fails the build by opting 120 static pages out), search working pre-hydration and without JS, and `generateStaticParams` on PDPs.

---

## 13. QA migration strategy

The 15 suites encode roughly 400 assertions, most of which record a real defect found and fixed. **Behavioural coverage must survive; selectors will not.** No script is deleted.

### Step 1 — Extract intent before touching code

Produce `scripts/qa/COVERAGE.md`: every assertion in every suite, as a behavioural statement independent of selector. *"Adding from a listing updates the header count"* — not *"`button.bg-cta` click increments `a[href='/cart']` aria-label"*. This is the artefact that must not be lost; it is cheap to write and expensive to reconstruct.

### Step 2 — Introduce the selector contract

Add `lib/testids.ts` and `scripts/qa/selectors.mjs` (the mirror). All migrated suites import from the mirror. A future redesign then edits one file instead of fifteen.

Preference order for targeting, strongest first: **ARIA role + accessible name** → `data-testid` → text content. **Never CSS classes.** Class-based targeting is what made the suites brittle.

### Step 3 — Classify each suite

| Suite | Fate | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `qa-purchase` (48) | **Port** | Journey is unchanged; checkout goes accordion → single page, so step transitions are rewritten |
| `qa-p1` (54) | **Port** | Facet *logic* is unchanged; facet *chrome* moves left-rail → top bar |
| `qa-discovery` (42) | **Port** | Results row → grid; PDP composition changes |
| `qa-home` (42) | **Rewrite** | Homepage IA changes wholesale; image/broken-asset checks port as-is |
| `qa-drawer` (69) | **Re-target** | Department drawer becomes `/browse` + popover. Modal-semantics assertions become popover semantics; single-instance assertions still apply |
| `qa-smoke-drawer` (49) | **Port** | Mini-cart behaviour is preserved; docked-vs-overlay adds a ≥1280 reflow assertion |
| `qa-smoke-final` (12) | **Port** | Highest value per line. Port first — it becomes the redesign's smoke gate |
| `qa-responsive` (189) | **Port nearly as-is** | Asserts overflow, escape-the-viewport and tap-target size generically — largely selector-independent. Add the bottom tab bar and safe areas |
| `qa-signin` (36), `qa-help` (26), `qa-fbt` (23) | **Port** | Local recomposition only |
| `visual-qa`, `audit-design`, `contact-sheet`, `qa-homepage-polish` | **Retarget** | `audit-design` gets new expected token values and becomes the design-system conformance gate |

### Step 4 — Sequence

1. Port `qa-smoke-final` **before** any redesign work. It is the "did I break the product" gate for every subsequent commit.
2. Port `qa-responsive` early — it is mostly selector-independent and catches the highest-risk class of redesign regression.
3. Port each remaining suite **in the same commit as the screen it covers**. A screen is not "done" until its suite is green.
4. Keep originals in `scripts/qa/legacy/` (moved, not deleted) until their replacement passes, then remove the legacy copy in a final cleanup commit.

### Step 5 — Parity gate

The redesign is not complete while any suite has fewer passing assertions than its predecessor. Record before/after counts per suite in `COVERAGE.md`.

---

## 14. Implementation order

Each wave leaves the app shippable and fully green. No wave ships a screen mixing old and new systems.

| Wave | Content | Why here |
|---|---|---|
| **0 — Foundations** | Design tokens in `app/globals.css`, fonts, `lib/testids.ts`, new `ui/` primitives (`Chip`, `Sheet`, `Overlay`, `Shelf`, `StatBar`, `Pager`, `Tabs`, `Skeleton`), restyle `Button`/`Badge`/`Field`. Port `qa-smoke-final` + `qa-responsive`. | Nothing visible ships; everything downstream depends on it |
| **1 — Chrome** | `AppBar`, `BrowsePopover`, `SearchOverlay`, `BottomTabs`, `NavRail`, footer. Retire `SubNav`/`DepartmentDrawer`/`DrawerTrigger`. Add `/browse`. Retarget `qa-drawer`. | Header is on every page; doing it first means every later screen is judged in its final frame |
| **2 — Cards & discovery** | `ProductCard`, `PriceBlock`, `Shelf`, `FacetBar` + filter `Sheet`, `Pager`, `/s` grid + density toggle. Port `qa-p1`, `qa-discovery`. | The card is the most-repeated component; settling it unblocks 3 and 4 |
| **3 — Homepage** | `PromoPair`, `CategoryMosaic`, guide band, recomposition. Retire `HeroCarousel`/`CardRow`/`ProductRail`. Rewrite `qa-home`. | Depends on `Shelf` and `ProductCard` from Wave 2 |
| **4 — PDP & reviews** | Two-column sticky, gallery, "At a glance", anchored tabs, reviews facts bar + cards, FBT. Port `qa-discovery` PDP half, `qa-fbt`. | Independent of 3; could run in parallel |
| **5 — Commerce** | Cart, mini-cart docking, single-page checkout, confirmation, orders. Port `qa-purchase`, `qa-smoke-drawer`. | Highest regression risk — land it when the system is settled |
| **6 — States** | U/V/W: skeletons, empty states, error states, 404, `/signin`, `/help`. Port `qa-signin`, `qa-help`. | Naturally last; touches every prior screen |
| **7 — Consistency** | Re-point `audit-design`, full regression, parity gate, legacy QA cleanup. | The Step-7 pass, repeated against the new system |

Phases B–G follow, unchanged from your plan.

---

## 15. Risks

**High**

1. **Scope.** Twenty-three areas is a full rebuild, not a polish pass. *Mitigation:* the wave order above, with the rule that no wave merges while a screen mixes old and new. A half-redesigned app reads worse to an evaluator than either the current one or the finished one.
2. **QA coverage loss.** ~400 assertions encode real, previously-shipped defects. Losing them silently re-opens fixed bugs. *Mitigation:* `COVERAGE.md` written **before** code changes; per-suite parity gate.
3. **Regressing the no-JS search path.** The overlay is JS-driven; search must still work pre-hydration and without JS. This has already been a shipped bug once. *Mitigation:* the overlay wraps a real GET form; the trigger degrades to a link to `/s`; `/s` carries an inline form. Asserted in the ported smoke suite.
4. **`useSearchParams` without `<Suspense>`.** New chrome (nav rail, facet bar, search overlay) all read search params. Any unguarded read opts all 120 static PDPs out of prerendering and **fails `next build`**. *Mitigation:* explicit boundary review in Waves 1–2; the build itself is the gate.

**Medium**

5. **Hydration mismatches.** New chrome reads persisted cart and auth state. Every such read must stay behind `useIsMounted()`. A past version of this pinned the cart badge to zero permanently.
6. **Mini-cart reflow at ≥1280px.** Reflowing the grid touches page-level layout and is the classic source of horizontal overflow. *Mitigation:* `qa-responsive` ported before Wave 5.
7. **Bottom tab bar collisions.** Overlaps sticky PDP and checkout bars; needs safe-area handling on iOS. *Mitigation:* a single `--bottom-chrome` height token that all sticky bottom elements respect.
8. **Font loading.** `next/font/google` requires network at build. *Mitigation:* self-host via `next/font/local`, consistent with the project's existing no-external-hosts rule.

**Low but worth stating**

9. **Familiarity cost.** Dropping the raised-symbol price, the pill button and the yellow CTA removes cues shoppers recognise. This is the assignment's explicit intent — but it is a real trade, not a free win.
10. **Imagery ceiling.** The catalogue is contextual stock photography, not cutouts on white. A cleaner, calmer design *raises* the visibility of weak product images. Some department tiles will look worse in the new system than the old one, and no layout change fixes that.
11. **Mini-cart churn.** The standing sidebar shipped days ago at your request. Its behaviour is preserved, but its composition changes again — flagging it so it isn't a surprise.

---

## 16. Acceptance criteria

The frontend redesign is complete when **all** of the following hold.

**Structural — the redesign is real, not a reskin**

- [ ] Header is one row ≤56px; the department sub-nav row no longer exists.
- [ ] Departments are reachable via `/browse` and a nav rail; `DepartmentDrawer`, `DrawerTrigger` and `navDrawer` store are deleted.
- [ ] Search is a full-screen overlay; it still works with JS disabled and before hydration.
- [ ] `/s` renders a grid by default, with a working density toggle.
- [ ] Filters render as a top facet bar plus one slide-over `Sheet` shared by desktop and mobile; no separate mobile filter drawer exists.
- [ ] Homepage has six modules; no autoplay carousel remains.
- [ ] PDP is two-column with a sticky decision card, an "At a glance" strip and anchored section nav.
- [ ] Checkout is a single page with a sticky summary; the three-step accordion is gone.
- [ ] Mobile has a bottom tab bar; no hamburger drawer remains.
- [ ] `HeroCarousel`, `CardRow`, `ProductRail`, `FilterShell`, `SubNav` are deleted, not merely unused.

**Visual system**

- [ ] `audit-design` reports: ≤7 heading sizes, ≤3 radii plus button radius, ≤2 button font sizes, ≤2 form-control families, one card padding.
- [ ] No screen renders `#131921`, `#232f3e`, `#ffd814`, `#f90` or any current Amazon-derived token.
- [ ] No pill-radius buttons; no raised currency symbol anywhere.
- [ ] Section rhythm is 48/32px, not uniform 16px.
- [ ] Every text/background pair passes WCAG AA at its rendered size.

**Functional parity**

- [ ] All 120 products, 672 reviews and 120 images render; zero broken images.
- [ ] Every URL parameter in `lib/search.ts` behaves identically; a filtered, sorted, paginated URL from before the redesign resolves to the same result set.
- [ ] Full journey completes: home → search → PDP → add → mini-cart → cart → checkout → confirmation → orders.
- [ ] Cart persists across reload; orders persist; cart clears after order.
- [ ] `computeTotals()` is the only source of money on screen — no formula duplicated in a component.
- [ ] `data/catalog.json`, `public/products/`, `data/image-sources.json`, `ATTRIBUTION.md` are byte-identical to `5d932a3`.
- [ ] `.agent-logs/` and `.claude/` unchanged; `.agent-logs/` not gitignored; project root not moved.

**QA**

- [ ] `scripts/qa/COVERAGE.md` exists and every legacy assertion maps to a ported one or a written justification for removal.
- [ ] Every ported suite passes with **≥** its original assertion count.
- [ ] `qa-responsive` passes at all seven widths, including the bottom tab bar and safe areas.
- [ ] No QA script targets a CSS class.
- [ ] `scripts/qa/legacy/` is empty and removed.

**Build**

- [ ] `npm run build` succeeds with 120 prerendered PDPs (no static-rendering bailout).
- [ ] `npm run typecheck` clean.
- [ ] No console or page errors on any route.

---

**Two decisions I need from you before Wave 0:**

1. **Display typeface.** Fraunces is my recommendation — a serif gives the strongest, cheapest identity break from Amazon. If you'd rather stay all-sans, say so now; it changes the whole system's character and is expensive to swap at Wave 3.
2. **Brand colour.** Deep teal `#14594F` is proposed. It's distinctive, reads as trustworthy in commerce, and passes contrast as a solid CTA. If you have a preference, Wave 0 is the only cheap moment to set it.

Nothing has been modified. Awaiting your go-ahead before Wave 0.

15 colours, 7 type roles with paired line-heights, two display families, named section rhythm (48/32px), 4 radii, 2 reserved shadows. Legacy replica tokens are **kept and annotated with the wave that retires each** — Wave 0 adds the new system without ripping out what current screens still reference.

## 5. Components restyled

All seven, props unchanged. Buttons are 8px rounded rectangles with a solid teal primary — no pills, no yellow. `PriceBlock` loses the raised currency symbol for tabular lining figures with an inline struck list price and a terracotta discount chip; its API and calculations are untouched. `StarRating` gains a `compact` form (one star, value, count) while keeping the full fractional row. `Field` drops its inset shadow and bespoke focus glow in favour of the app-wide focus ring.

## 6. Test-ID contract

11 live IDs wired, 9 declared and reserved for later waves. Deliberately small — most of the app is reachable by role and accessible name, so an ID earns its place only where semantics can't distinguish (one card among sixteen, one money figure among several). The smoke suite asserts live IDs exist **and** that reserved ones are not wired early, which is the only mechanism that can catch the TS map and the JS mirror drifting apart.

## 7–8. QA coverage and ports

`COVERAGE.md` documents all 15 suites as behavioural statements with call-site counts, runtime counts, fate and target wave. Two suites ported, both **above** parity:

| Suite | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| smoke-final | 12 | **19/19** |
| responsive | 189 | **210/210** |

Neither gained by weakening anything. Smoke added the contract checks, a mini-cart/cart subtotal agreement check, and an order-total-exceeds-subtotal check. Responsive gained a tenth route.

## 9–12. Results

typecheck clean · build succeeds with **all 120 PDPs prerendered** · smoke **19/19** · responsive **210/210** · all 7 routes return 200 · zero console or page errors · no horizontal overflow at any of the seven widths.

## 13. Issues and deferred items

**The font decision changed under me, and I think correctly.** You asked me to report before making an unnecessary change, so: the project already used `next/font/google` for Inter, and I verified the build output contains no Google host reference — it self-hosts. I added Fraunces the same way as the minimal change. **The build then failed**: `next/font/google` must fetch at build time, and adding a family invalidated the cached download. Earlier builds had only been working off that cache. So I did what you'd asked for as the preferred option — downloaded both latin variable fonts, committed them to `public/fonts/`, and switched to `next/font/local`. Builds now need no network at all, matching the rule the imagery already follows. Both are SIL OFL 1.1, recorded in `public/fonts/LICENSE-OFL.txt` (I did not touch `ATTRIBUTION.md`).

**Two real accessibility defects surfaced**, not port artifacts. The old suite's threshold was 22px; the 24px minimum you asked for caught the PDP breadcrumb links (14×20) and the cart Delete button (39×20). Fixed with the `.tap-target` utility, which extends the hit area without changing appearance. That in turn exposed a flaw in my own check — `getBoundingClientRect` can't see a pseudo-element's hit area — so the check now probes what a finger actually lands on via `elementFromPoint`. That's a stronger test than the one it replaces.

**Deferred, documented not stubbed:** bottom-tab-bar and safe-area assertions (`PENDING wave 1`), mini-cart reflow at ≥1280px (`PENDING wave 5`).

**Known temporary state:** the app is mixed-era by design — new primitives inside the old composition, so the header is still navy while CTAs are teal. Wave 1 closes the largest part of that gap. Base line-height stays 14/20 rather than the system's 14/21, because changing the root reflows every existing screen and that is a redesign, not a foundation change.

## One concern that must be resolved before Wave 1

**`app/not-found.tsx` still imports `Inter` from `next/font/google`.**

```
app/not-found.tsx:3:  import { Inter } from "next/font/google";
app/not-found.tsx:9:  const inter = Inter({ subsets: ["latin"], variable: "--font-inter", display: "swap" });
```

This is **pre-existing** — it's in the checkpoint and Wave 0 did not touch that file — but Wave 0 is what makes it matter, and I missed it. Consequences:

1. **The build-time network dependency Wave 0 was meant to remove is still there.** The build passing right now proves nothing: `.next/cache` still holds the Inter that `next/font/google` downloaded before. The earlier Wave 0 build failure named this exact file in its import trace.
2. **A clean build with no route to Google Fonts will fail** — fresh clone, CI, or Vercel. That is the failure mode that forced the switch to local fonts in the first place.
3. It defines a **second, conflicting `--font-inter`**, which is why a stale hashed woff2 is still being requested on pages built from the old cache.

I did not fix it, because this pass is read-only. The fix is small and belongs at the start of Wave 1: point `not-found.tsx` at the same `localFont` instances as `app/layout.tsx` (or drop its own font block, since it already imports `globals.css`). I'd also suggest one clean-build check (`rm -rf .next && npm run build`) afterwards to prove the network dependency is genuinely gone — the current green build cannot prove it.

Everything else is clean and Wave 1 can proceed once that's addressed.
